NexPoint RE Finance (NREF) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Shrinking multifamily supply and rising life science leases shape next moves.

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

NexPoint Real Estate Finance, Inc. (NREF -7.17%) reported third quarter results characterized by stable dividend coverage and the tactical deployment of capital into high-yielding residential and life science credit. Management reported that the company is actively developing a supplemental capital program to address liquidity needs for sponsors of agency-underwritten multifamily debt facing extension tests. The company stated it maintains a clean capital structure with significant dry powder following a preferred stock offering and an upsized credit facility. Management indicated that the current supply-demand dynamics in multifamily, marked by a significant drop in new construction starts, are expected to restore pricing power as the pipeline bottoms in 2027.

Operator: Hello, everyone. Thank you for joining us, and welcome to NexPoint Real Estate Finance Q3 2026 Earnings Call. After today's prepared remarks, we will host a question-and-answer session. If you would like to ask a question, please press star one to raise your hand. To withdraw your question, press star one again. I will now hand the conference over to Carley Musser with Communications and Corporate Affairs. Carley, please go ahead.

Carley Musser: Thank you, Carley. Good morning, and welcome to NexPoint Real Estate Finance's conference call to review the company's results for the third quarter ended September 30, 2026. On the call today are Paul Richards, Executive Vice President and Chief Financial Officer, and Matt McGraner, Executive Vice President and Chief Investment Officer. As a reminder, this call is being webcast through the company's website at nref.nexpoint.com. Before we begin, I would like to remind everyone that this conference call contains forward-looking statements within the meaning of the Private Securities Litigation Reform Act of 1995 that are based on management's current expectations, assumptions, and beliefs.

Listeners should not place undue reliance on any forward-looking statements and are encouraged to review the company's annual report on Form 10-K and the company's other filings with the SEC for a more complete discussion of risks and other factors that could affect the forward-looking statements. The statements made during this conference call speak only as of today's date, and except as required by law, NREF does not undertake any obligation to publicly update or revise any forward-looking statements. This conference call also includes an analysis of non-GAAP financial measures. For a more complete discussion of these non-GAAP financial measures, see the company's presentation that was filed earlier today.

I would now like to turn the call over to Paul Richards. Please go ahead, Paul.

Paul Richards: Thanks, Carley, and good morning, everyone. I'll walk through our quarterly results, cover the balance sheet, and provide guidance for Q4 before turning it over to Matt for a deeper dive on the portfolio and the macro lending environment. For the third quarter, we reported a net loss of $0.65 per diluted share compared to $1.14 for Q3 2025. Earnings available for distribution was $0.52 per diluted share in Q3 compared to $0.51 per diluted share in the same period of 2025. Cash available for distribution was $0.60 per share in Q3 compared to $0.53 per diluted share in the same period of 2025.

We paid a regular dividend of $0.50 per share in the third quarter, which was 1.2x covered by the cash available for distribution. On October 26, 2026, the board declared a dividend of $0.50 per share payable for the fourth quarter of 2026. Book value per diluted share decreased 5.2% from Q2 2026 to $17.63 per diluted share, primarily driven by unrealized losses on a few of our fair valued positions due to a rapid rise in rates over the quarter, which has subsequently retraced and rallied back a substantial amount, which we believe should assist in a marked rebound for Q4. Turning to new investments during the quarter.

We have continued to originate new investments across our target asset classes, funded through a combination of retained operating cash flow, proceeds from our Series C Preferred offering, and additional capacity under our secured financing facilities, reflecting our continued ability to identify and execute attractive opportunities that drive returns for our shareholders. We funded a $4 million preferred equity investment in a multifamily property that pays a monthly coupon of 14%, a $2.2 million preferred equity investment in a marina at a 13% coupon, and funded an additional $91.7 million on other existing commitments in the quarter.

During the quarter, we upsized our $375 million term loan facility and total return swap with Mizuho Capital Markets to $450 million, which we used to fund existing commitments, primarily an $80.5 million bridge loan yielding 14% that paid off at the end of the quarter. In accordance with the payoff, we paid down $52.2 million on the secured facility, lowering total outstanding balance to $397.8 million. Combined with the $27.9 million we raised in our Series C Preferred, we head into the last quarter of 2026 with what we believe to be one of the cleanest, most flexible capital structures in the commercial mortgage REIT sector.

Our portfolio is comprised of 83 investments with a total outstanding balance of $1 billion. Our investments are allocated across sectors as follows: 40.7% life sciences, 38.3% multifamily, 14% single family rental, 4.6% storage, 2.2% industrial, and 0.2% marina. Our fixed income portfolio is allocated across investments as follows: 28.4% preferred equity investments, 22.8% mezzanine loans, 18.2% revolving credit facilities, 17.9% CMBS B-pieces, 6.4% senior loans, 3.9% IO strips, and 2.3% promissory notes.

The assets collateralizing our investments are allocated geographically as follows: 31.1% Massachusetts, 15% Texas, 7% North Carolina, 6.5% Florida, and 5.5% California, with the remainder across states with less than 5% exposure, reflecting our heavy preference for Sunbelt markets, with Massachusetts and California exposure heavily weighted towards life science. The collateral on our portfolio is 78.3% stabilized, with 64.7% loan-to-value and a weighted average DSCR of 1.39x. We have $913.1 million of debt outstanding with a weighted average cost of 5.8% and has a weighted average maturity of two and half years. Our secured debt is collateralized by $1.5 billion of collateral with a weighted average maturity of 2.6 years and a debt-to-equity ratio of 0.97x.

Moving to guidance for the fourth quarter. Earnings available for distribution, $0.42 per diluted share at the midpoint, with a range of $0.37 on the low end and $0.47 on the high end. Cash available for distribution, $0.55 per diluted share at the midpoint, with a range of $0.50 on the low end and $0.60 on the high end. With that, I would like to turn it over to Matt for a detailed discussion of the portfolio and the current market environment. Matt?

Matt McGraner: Thank you, Paul. This quarter was a solid, consistent execution. Both EAD and CAD came in ahead of our guidance. Given our positive credit profile and the improving but still challenging macro backdrop, we continue to play offense in this environment and expect opportunities for our solution-oriented capital to grow over the near-term. The Fed held its target rate at 3.75%-4% in late October, and the 10-year Treasury moved back above 5%. Higher for longer pressures borrowers who need to refinance or meet extension tests, and that is exactly where disciplined solution capital earns its pricing power. Our new investments are priced at double-digit coupons at reset basis, and therefore, we believe we are well-positioned for this type of environment.

Turning to our verticals. Residential, between multi and single-family rental, remains our largest exposure. Our multifamily credit sits predominantly behind agency-underwritten senior debt, which has historically been some of the most resilient collateral in real estate. Freddie Mac's aggregate losses have averaged about 7 basis points per year since 2009, with $432.6 million of cumulative losses on $645.8 billion of issuance through August of 2026. That is not to say there is not stress in the system, though. Trepp has Freddie's multifamily delinquency at 80 basis points of UPB in August, up from 48 basis points in August of 2024. We see this as an opportunity, however, which I will elaborate on in a minute.

On the supply side, multifamily construction starts fell more than 40% between 2023 and 2025, and new deliveries are forecasted to bottom near 327,000 units in 2027, well below the roughly 600,000-unit peak in 2024. Supply is what broke pricing power over the last two years, and the falling supply pipeline is what we expect to restore it. That said, the recovery is not linear, and in the third quarter, blended lease rates across our portfolios remained flat to slightly positive, and concessions, while abating, are still prevalent in the market for Class A and Class B multifamily in our geographical footprint.

The good news is we are underwriting and deploying our capital into new residential investments on today's rents, again, on reset basis and not on a recovery pro forma. Our single-family rental book continues to perform really well, with significant equity cushion. Overall, we view the sector's credit profile as slightly more favorable than multifamily, with rents already in positive territory and less pockets of distress. The number of households renting single-family homes continue to rise, and higher for longer should keep demand high for our affordable SFR in our markets. Now on to life science.

At LYFE, the sponsor's recap process is underway, and we continue to expect a substantial, if not all, of our return of capital in the next quarter or two. When the capital comes back, our plan is to redeploy most of it into residential collateral and to move life science back toward roughly one-third of the portfolio over time. Across the rest of our life science exposure, fundamentals continue to improve sequentially, particularly in Boston and San Francisco, with 220,000 sq ft of new leases signed during the year-to-date. The remaining industry-wide speculative pipeline is expected to deliver through 2026, after which new supply will be limited largely to fully pre-leased build-to-suit projects.

Demand is broadening beyond traditional lab users to biomanufacturing, onshoring, and AI-driven users that need power density, floor loads, and lab-grade infrastructure. Importantly, and again, most of this book was originated in 2025 through 2026 at a reset basis. In self-storage, fundamentals troughed in 2025, and with new construction pipelines well below historical averages, we expect conditions to keep strengthening. During the quarter, we continued to add small mezzanine positions on newly stabilized storage assets at double-digit coupons. The sector has already largely reported better-than-expected results during the quarter, and our NSP-owned portfolio continues to perform very well also, with occupancy as of 9/30 at 93.5% and rents up 600 basis points year-over-year.

On the pipeline in April, we outlined more than $190 million of NREF investments and more than $225 million of structured product opportunities. Year-to-date, I'm pleased to say we have closed $280 million of that $415 million pipeline and have another $160 million committed over the next 12 months with an active pipeline backlog building. We were deliberately selective in the third quarter, and with the additional Mizuho capacity and expected repayments, we have the dry powder to lean in at attractive spreads in the fourth quarter and into 2027. I'm glad we waited on some of the resi opportunities because more will appear.

One critical need for the market will be to provide solution and special situation capital for the residential sector, and this is a great opportunity for NREF over the next couple of years, with the caveat that it might not fully materialize if the capital markets rapidly improve, which we wouldn't hate either. The stress in agency multifamily is concentrated in floating rate loans originated in 2020 through 2023, when SOFR was near zero. Those loans need replacement rate caps, cap escrows, and in many cases, a paydown to meet their refi and extension test, and a meaningful number of sponsors do not have the equity to do it.

In agency floating rate securitizations alone, that vintage is more than $32 billion across roughly 1,000 loans, and about a quarter of that balance is already on special servicer watch lists. We have demonstrated time-and time again that we can be a creative partner with the agencies, particularly Freddie, to provide solution-oriented capital on a bespoke basis. We're already developing a program where NREF would provide co-terminous supplemental capital behind the existing first lien so that sponsors can buy the cap, fund reserves, and pass their tests rather than hand back the keys. This is exactly the kind of solution capital we have built the platform around.

It sits behind conservatively underwritten agency senior debt in an asset class and in markets we know as operators, and it is priced like rescue capital. We would also expect it to be financed on a matched term basis, similar to how our original SFR portfolio was financed. The program is not final, but we do believe there is a strong need for it, and we are excited about it. To close and summarize, we are pleased with where we are, particularly in this cycle. Earnings came in ahead of guidance, the dividend is covered, credit continues to hold well, and our balance sheet has more capacity than it did a year ago.

As always, I want to thank this team for their hard work and focus, and with that, we would like to turn the call over to the operator and take your questions.

Operator: We will now begin the question and answer session. If you would like to ask a question, please press star one to raise your hand. To withdraw your question, press star one again. We ask that you pick up your handset when asking a question to allow for optimum sound quality. If you are muted locally, please remember to unmute your device. Please stand by while we compile the Q&A roster. Your first question comes from the line of Jade Rahmani with KBW. Your line is open. Please go ahead.

Jade Rahmani: Thank you very much. Could you give an update on the IQHQ exposure, specifically the credit facility and preferred equity position, and how you expect that to perform over the next one to two years?

Matt McGraner: Yeah. Happy to. Good morning. The preferred and the RCF sit at different detachment points, but it's roughly, I think, $350 million collectively. The company is, as I've said in the past, executing on its plan, seeing a lot of good traffic in Boston and San Francisco in particular. The near-term, I would say, catalyst is Alewife, and they're continuing to either recap or monetize and/or sell that asset. To the extent that it's sold, then that frees up a ton of capital to delever the company and take out some of our capital.

In terms of, I'd say, the widening funnel, one thing that's popped up in the last quarter or two, these assets have not only really great infrastructure, but they unbeknownst to the sponsor has a great deal of powered shell. What I mean by that is, the assets are now starting to get looks for edge compute, and then the economics of that are pretty stellar. That's one sort of green shoot that has appeared relatively recently that I think the team is working on and could be a pretty interesting monetization event, given where we are in the cycle.

But, yeah, I think our exposure is, from a per square foot and a detachment standpoint, some of the best out there. I think the RCF detachment point is roughly $1,100 a sq ft. The series E detachment point is roughly $1,350 a sq ft. If you compare those bases to the rest of the industry, and particularly, just plain new office rents and new office buildings, I think we like where we are in the cap stack.

Jade Rahmani: If Alewife refinanced and there was a repayment, would that pay down any of the preferred or credit facility?

Matt McGraner: Yeah, it absolutely could. The current or our current first mortgage is roughly a couple hundred million. I know there's term sheets and other kind of loan sizing out there in the 400-ish range. So, yeah, I would expect there to be excess cash there.

Because you did mention that there's significant challenges in multifamily credit. I know the vintage is more recent, but if you could comment on that portfolio.

Matt McGraner: Yeah. The problems that we had were like two or three loans that we've already taken back and consolidated. One comes to mind was the Alexander project in Atlanta that we took back, I think, early last year. That project is now 97% leased, 95% occupied, and doing really well. Same thing with Ridgeview, and then I think another deal called Mag & May. But all of those were known to us and have largely been foreclosed on and now owned by us, and we're nursing them back to health, which kind of all told isn't a ton of exposure. The rest of the mezz and pref positions are performing well and on schedule.

The new construction pref and mezz positions are doing extremely well and delivering kind of on schedule, on budget, and then hopefully into an environment in 2029 where there's going to be no new supply. So, feel really good about that book, particularly relative to the rest of the commercial real estate mortgage REIT universe.

Breece Hall up to full practice Thursday, says he's ready to play this week

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

It looks like running back Breece Hall will be back in the Jets lineup this week.

Hall practiced in full for the first time since suffering the quad injury that has kept him out for the last five games. Hall said after practice that he is ready to play against the Chiefs.

Braelon Allen has impressed during Hall’s absence and is coming off a season-high 147 yards against the Raiders. Offensive coordinator Frank Reich was asked about how the team will balance the workload during a Thursday press conference.

“We have some plays that are tagged for one or the other, but it’s a great problem to have,” Reich said, via a transcript from the team. “You got two number one backs. Breece is obviously the bell cow, he’s our guy, but it’ll be a mix. And as far as hot hand, not hot hand, I think that’s always an in-game feel thing, it really is. My experience in it, because I’ve had that experience, I know I haven’t had two guys like this, but yeah, it’s just an in-game feel thing.”

Linebacker David Bailey (groin), linebacker Joseph Ossai (ankle), linebacker Demario Davis (rest), safety VJ Payne (knee), and tight end Mason Taylor (neck) also moved up to full participation. Defensive lineman Harrison Phillips (ankle), linebacker Trevin Wallace (groin), and wide receiver Garrett Wilson (concussion) remained out of practice.

Meet *sshole, the vulnerability that lets hackers SSH into Linux devices

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Certain configurations of Linux are affected. Other distros may be as well.

Devices running some configurations of Alpine Linux, and possibly other distributions, are affected by a decade-old vulnerability that allows attackers to log into devices with no password.

The vulnerability exists in the interaction between OpenSSH, the code library implementing SSH connections, and musl, a lightweight alternative to the libc library that, unlike most other Linux distributions, is used in Alpine. Further, OpenSSH using musl (pronounced muscle) must not use PAM (pluggable authentication modules), a framework that handles user authentication and account-access checks. Alpine is a lightweight, widely used Linux distribution. Other platforms may be affected if they use the same configurations.

There are two other conditions necessary for successful exploitation. First, a system must have at least one user account that’s accessible using a password that is hashed using the SHA256 or SHA512 functions. Second, the target system account must be configured to be passwordless, by way of specifying a “*” in the password field in /etc/shadow, the Linux file where password hashes are stored. Passwordless accounts are commonly used for accounts that use certificate-based authentication. This setting is common for certain types of accounts.

When all the conditions are met, the consequences are high.

“An attacker who can reach your SSH port can log in without knowing any password,” Alpine maintainers said Thursday. “It takes one connection, works every time, and needs no retries.”

When logging into a passwordless account, the attacker can gain access by inputting more than 256 characters in the password field.

Security firm Checkpoint said that the use of the SHA256 or SHA512 functions “matters because sshd borrows that account’s salt. Without it, the attack fails.”  Company researchers said their testing also showed that the account must be active, not just hold a hash. Checkpoint further reported that root accounts are safe under the default PermitRootLogin prohibit password setting, though reachable accounts with doas or sudo rights can still become root.

Despite the prerequisites, *sshole—the vulnerability name given by the person who discovered it—likely threatens large numbers of systems. The Docker Hub base image for Alpine has more than 1 billion downloads. Alpine has long been popular in routers, smart TVs, industrial controllers, and other types of embedded devices, many of which can’t be easily updated, if at all. More recently, Alpine has been adopted for use in desktops and servers. Alpine is also used in many container images.

According to security firm Detectify, Shodan lists around 22 million systems with SSH open on port 22. Only a fraction meet the conditions necessary for exploitation, but given the ease and speed of testing each one, locating vulnerable devices won’t require much effort or time.

It’s not entirely clear where the vulnerability resides. OpenSSH, which issued an update Thursday, said: “We consider the root cause of this bug to be outside sshd, but it is exploitable via sshd and can be avoided there.” Musl developer Rich Felker, meanwhile, wrote: “It is my position that this is not a bug in musl just an erroneous use by the caller.” Felker went on to say that musl developers have wanted to make changes to the mechanism “for a while now” and planned to do so in an upcoming release. Alpine has also issued an update.

It’s not yet known what other packages may be vulnerable. The person who discovered *sshole said OpenWRT is affected, but by default, that OS uses the Dropbear package, not OpenSSH, for SSH connections (though maintainers provide instructions for installing the latter). The discoverer also names the packages buildroot and yocto as being affected, but that isn’t yet confirmed.

Despite the uncertainty, anyone using a Linux system that uses both musl and OpenSSH should take time to immediately investigate if they’re affected. A good starting point is here. Both Alpine and OpenSSH patches should be installed on all affected systems. There is no known CVE designation for the vulnerability.

Ars Technica has been separating the signal from the noise for over 25 years. With our unique combination of technical savvy and wide-ranging interest in the technological arts and sciences, Ars is the trusted source in a sea of information. After all, you don’t need to know everything, only what’s important.

Hudson Pacific (HPP) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Occupancy rose a fifth straight quarter as debt fell and studios held steady.

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

Hudson Pacific Properties, Inc. (HPP -17.05%) reported a period of active leasing and balance sheet management as it navigated shifting demand in West Coast office and studio markets. Management reported that the company prioritized debt reduction through strategic asset dispositions and the opportunistic repurchase of senior notes while maintaining a focus on high-quality assets in technology-driven submarkets. The company stated that leasing activity was primarily concentrated in the San Francisco Bay Area, supported by the ongoing recovery in the technology and venture capital sectors. Furthermore, management indicated that the studio segment showed signs of stabilization with improved production spending in New York and potential legislative catalysts in California.

Operator: Hello, everyone. Thank you for joining us, and welcome to the Hudson Pacific Properties third quarter 26 earnings call. After today's prepared remarks, we will host a question and answer session. If you would like to ask a question, please press *1 to raise your hand. To withdraw your question, press 1 again. I will now hand the conference over to Laura Campbell, executive vice president, investor relations and marketing. Laura, please go ahead.

Laura Campbell: Good morning, everyone. Thanks for joining us. With me on the call today are Victor J. Coleman, chairman and CEO, Mark T. Lammas, President; Harout Krikor Diramerian, CFO and Arthur X. Suazo, EVP of Leasing. This morning, we filed our earnings release and supplemental on an 8-K with the SEC and both are now available on our website along with an audio webcast of this call for replay. Some of the information we will share today on the call is forward looking in nature. Please reference our earnings release and supplemental for statements regarding forward looking information. As well as the reconciliation of non GAAP financial measures used on this call.

Today, Victor will focus on current industry and market trends. Mark will provide detail on our office leasing and studio operations, and Harout will review our financial results and 2026 outlook. Thereafter, we will be happy to take your questions. Victor?

Victor J. Coleman: Thanks, Laura. Good morning, everyone, and welcome to our third quarter call. This was another quarter of steady progress for Hudson Pacific. We signed 534 thousand square feet of new and renewal office leases and our in service office occupancy grew for the 5th consecutive quarter approaching 86%. Together with improved studio performance, that contributed to growth of 8% in same store cash NOI and a core FFO per share year over year, and we are once again raising our full year outlook. We also continue to execute on our disposition plan and strengthen our balance sheet.

Pro forma for our fourth quarter asset sales and senior notes repurchase total liquidity stands at $840 million with our share of debt reduced by 14% year-over-year. And looking ahead, our leasing pipeline stands at 2.1 million square feet with 1.1 million square feet now in advanced stages nearly twice the level of a year ago. A pipeline of similar size set the stage for a very strong 2026. And its greater maturity today further positions us to extend our earnings growth into 2027. Touching on our markets, I am going to begin with the capital fueling demand. US venture backed companies raised $98.4 billion in the third quarter.

While below the outsized first half totals it was roughly double the quarterly levels of 2023 and 2024. Year to date investment now exceeds $515 billion already more than 60% above full-year 2025. With over 70% of it deployed in our West Coast markets. AI remains the primary driver accounting for 20 US rounds of $1 billion or more in the quarter while software also has captured a record share of venture dollars this year. Several indicators support continued funding. US VC fundraising reached over $100 billion through the third quarter already ahead of all of 2025 and a record acquisition exit values are recycling capital back into the ecosystem.

We are mindful of the recent volatility in tech stocks, which is largely centered on the capital spending of the largest platforms, that is a different part of the AI economy from the 1 driving demand in our markets. We will continue to watch how public market sentiments affect private funding, but so far, we have not seen it in our pipeline or tour activity. On the ground, demand continues to build. AI and tech driven leasing is broadening the office recovery across the West Coast with virtually no new supply coming online.

San Francisco posted its 8th consecutive quarter of positive net absorption adding 1.3 million square feet Citywide vacancy has improved 660 basis points over the past year, and asking rents are up nearly 10%. The South Financial District continues to lead having absorbed more than 2 million square feet so far this year. With large blocks in the South of Market quickly disappearing, we expect tenants to widen their searches into adjacent submarkets which should benefit our assets greatly. Silicon Valley delivered its strongest leasing quarter since 2019. At 4.1 million square feet It also absorbed more than 1 million square feet and active tenant demand rose to 12.4 million square feet with Santa Clara among the leading submarkets.

On the peninsula, net absorption turned positive led by Redwood City, Redwood Shores at more than 40% of the region's total, while neighboring Palo Alto remains 1 of the Bay Area's tightest office markets with rents among the highest in the country. Further north, in Seattle, net absorption reached its highest level since 2021. While Downtown Seattle recorded its first positive quarter in nearly 5 years on the strength of the CBD. Pioneer Square has been positive both for the quarter and the year to date. Los Angeles by contrast remains more mixed. Leasing volume rose nearly 12% year-over-year, but net absorption was modestly negative. West Los Angeles captured roughly 40% of all leases.

In Los Angeles while with Brentwood posting positive absorption this quarter. And in Vancouver, class a vacancy downtown fell for the 5th straight quarter to its lowest level since 2022 with no new downtown supply expected until at least 2032. Conditions are setting up for upward pressure on premium rents. Turning to our studio business, production levels in Los Angeles and New York continue to move in different directions. But as Mark is going to discuss, our prime locations are performing well in both. Third Quarter Los Angeles on location shoot days were 5% lower year-over-year and remained 29% below the 5-year average.

New York, meanwhile, is surging with third quarter production spending up 87% year-over-year to $1.6 billion approaching California's $1.8 billion. Filming permits in New York City were up 27% year-to-date as of September. Looking ahead, see potential catalysts for Los Angeles production At the federal level, legislation introduced this fall would create a 20% to 30% film and TV tax credit that stacks on top of the existing state credits and supporters are pushing to pass this before year end. In a bold move this week, governor Newsom proposed raising the state's annual credit from $750 million to at least $1 billion and for the first time extending eligibility to above the line compensation. Closing the gap with competing states.

This proposal will require legislative action and will likely carry into the next administration, but it is a clear signal of the state's commitment to bringing production back. We are also continuing to sharpen the portfolio through opportunistic asset sales and year to date, we closed nearly $190 million of non-core, FFO-accretive dispositions. That includes $90.5 million from the sales of 2001 Gateway and 75 and 99 Howard in the third quarter. 75.5 million of sales for the Skyport Plaza in the fourth quarter and $23 million of sales of the non core assets from Coyote across both quarters.

The office sales took largely vacant capital intensive assets off the books allowing us to focus on capital leasing at our highest potential properties. Consistent with our capital allocation priorities, we use the majority of the net proceeds to delever, which Harout is going to discuss shortly. As we refine the portfolio, its quality continues to be recognized externally. We were named as a GRESB sector leader for the 6th consecutive year and earned a 5-star rating in the 2026 real estate assessment. We also ranked first among US listed office companies for the 2nd consecutive year and 1st among all listed participants in The Americas.

That recognition reflects the investments we have made to keep our portfolio young, modern, efficient, and resilient, as well as our management team's commitment to quality and discipline. Now with that, I am going to hand it over to Mark to talk through leasing and operational details.

Mark T. Lammas: Thanks, Victor. We signed 74 leases totaling approximately 534 thousand square feet in the third quarter with activity evenly balanced between new leasing and renewals. Tech tenants provided a significant source of demand at 28% of activity roughly 2-thirds of our leasing was in the Bay Area where Palo Alto led all submarkets at over 110 thousand square feet. This included a 65 thousand-square-foot renewal and expansion lease with Rivian and Volkswagen Group Technologies at Clock Tower Square which addressed 1 of the 2028 expirations over 50 thousand square feet. Our in service occupancy increased 310 basis points sequentially to 85.6%, our 5th consecutive quarter of gains and our lease rate climbed 330 basis points to 86.1%.

Adjusted for asset sales and the temporary removal of Fourth & Traction in our Los Angeles Arts District from our in service portfolio, our occupancy and leased rate increased 80 and 100 basis points, respectively on a like for like basis. Regarding Fourth & Traction, the City of Los Angeles adopted seismic retrofit requirements several years ago that applied to the building, so this is work we needed to do. PayPal's recent expiration gives us a window to complete it and we have several prospective long term tenants with requirements of up to 75 thousand square feet interested in the building. that is precisely why we are moving quickly now so the work gets done before they move in.

We expect to finish the seismic work by Q2, at a rent spread of negative 5.6% on a GAAP basis and negative 0.4% on a cash basis. Driven by new and renewal leasing at our Peninsula and Silicon Valley assets. Third quarter net effective rents increased 30% year-over-year, and on a trailing 12-month basis were up 9%. Tour activity remained strong with 116 tours covering nearly 2 million square feet in the third quarter. Tour count was up 13% year-over-year, and the average requirement of 17 thousand square feet remains well above the roughly 10 thousand square feet typical in 2023 and 2024.

Average requirement sizes grew most on the Peninsula and in Silicon Valley where they roughly doubled this quarter, and in Seattle where they rose almost 50%. Even after signing 2.4 million square feet year to date, up 40% from last year, our pipeline has remained above 2 million square feet for 7 consecutive quarters. Roughly 25% above our long-term average. Almost 60% of the pipeline is new leases supporting continued occupancy gains while the renewal portion has grown to over 800 thousand square feet the highest level we have tracked. And as Victor noted, more than 1 million square feet is in late stage negotiations.

The quality and momentum of our pipeline are as strong as we have seen and we are highly confident in our ability to convert a significant portion of it. Turning to our expirations. We have approximately 50% coverage on both the remaining 144 thousand square feet expiring in 2026 and the 1.1 million square feet expiring in 2027. We define coverage as deals and leases, LOIs, or proposals. For the first half of 27, coverage is over 50% including full coverage on the Salesforce and Twilio space at Rincon Center in San Francisco, where we are in late stage negotiations.

Second half 27 expirations are mostly smaller leases, averaging about 6 thousand square feet, and those tenants typically do not engage until about a quarter before expiration. At Washington 1 thousand consistent with last quarter, we have coverage on approximately 65% of the building with active negotiations across 10 tenants whose requirements range up to 125 thousand square feet, including over 60 thousand square feet of late stage deals. Activity at our move in ready spec suites has increased meaningfully since their June completion and plans and pricing are complete for the next phase of spec suites on the 5th and 6th floors.

Beyond our current pipeline, tour activity has picked up noticeably from tenants with near term occupancy needs most of them new to the market. As has been reported, while Weil, Gotshal & Manges, our tenant at Towers at Shores Center in Redwood Shores is winding down operations. Weil occupied 89 thousand square feet across 2 leases. 38 thousand square feet expired after quarter end and the remaining 51 thousand square feet runs through 2038. While recently completed an estimated $700 per square foot build out leaving pristine space that ranks among the Peninsula's best for professional service firms. With in place rents at market.

Beyond the public announcements, we have not heard from Weil about its plans for the Redwood Shores lease. Given the quality of the space and the demand for both the asset and Redwood Shores more broadly, we are confident we could release it efficiently. In our studio segment, our in service stages were 74.7% leased on a trailing 3-month basis in the third quarter stable compared to the prior quarter. Our Hollywood stages remained well leased at 95% and Sunset Pier 94 continued to ramp with its lease rate up another 370 basis points to 82.2%.

In contrast to the broader Los Angeles market, our prime location stages in production fleet saw higher production activity and spend compared to the same quarter last year, though activity softened from the last quarter. Together with lower operating expenses, both sequentially and year over year, and the continued wind down of noncore Coyote operations, our share of core studio NOI improved to $4.3 million from $2.2 million last quarter and up considerably from negative $2.5 million a year ago. Lastly, in terms of additional avenues for value creation, after quarter end, we secured entitlements to convert approximately 164 thousand square feet of office space at 901 Market in San Francisco into 136 residential units above the existing retail.

As we did at 10.9 thousand Washington, we have created real optionality giving us multiple paths to respond to market demand and maximize value. We are advancing construction drawings and evaluating pricing and we will determine the best path forward in the coming quarters. This remains part of a broader effort as we continue to evaluate residential and mixed use opportunities alongside our office assets in Palo Alto, and across the peninsula. And now Harout will take you through our financial results and outlook.

Harout Krikor Diramerian: Thanks, Mark. In the third quarter, total revenues were $180.9 million compared to $180.6 million in the prior year primarily due to asset dispositions most significantly the sale of Element LA, partially offset by improved office occupancy and increased studio production activity. G&A improved 10% to $12.3 million compared to $13.7 million in the prior year resulting from cost cutting initiatives. Primarily driven by noncash impairments of Skyport Plaza and Hill 7 and a loss on the sale of 875 And 899 Howard.

Deconsolidation loss related to Sunset Glen Oaks accounted for the loss reported in the prior year period. Excluding these items, our underlying performance continued to strengthen. Core FFO grew to $18.6 million, up approximately 11% from $16.7 million in the prior year and increased approximately 8% on a per diluted share basis to $0.28 from $0.26. This was driven by higher same store NOI across both our office and studio properties and lower non real estate depreciation from the wind down of noncore Coyote operations, partially offset by asset sales. Adjustments to FFO totaled negative $2.8 million or negative $0.04 per diluted share primarily reflecting gains on non core Coyote lease terminations.

A prior period property tax refund, and the sale of nonreal estate assets partially offset by noncore Coyote costs. This compares to positive $2 million or $0.03 per diluted share in the prior year. We grew same-store cash NOI 8.3% to $85.2 million, compared to $78.7 million in the prior year. Driven by improved office occupancy and increased studio production activity. Turning to the balance sheet. We ended the quarter with $956 million of liquidity, including $161 million of unrestricted cash and full availability of $795 million on our revolver. Net debt to undepreciated book value was 32.1%, and our debt was 100% fixed or capped. At a weighted average interest rate of 4.9%.

Providing additional flexibility as we continue to execute our business plan for the portfolio. The extension required no principal pay down. The spread is unchanged, and we fixed SOFR at 3.5% through the extended maturity slightly better than the swaps it replaced. Subsequent to the quarter end, we repurchased $200 million of senior notes $100 million each of our 2027 and 2028 notes at a discount to par. Beyond the senior notes, our remaining 27 maturities are nonrecourse, Property level joint venture loans primarily the Hollywood Media portfolio CMBS, which we just extended.

For our 2027 and 2028 maturities, we expect to utilize a combination of liquidity, asset sales, and capital markets in a disciplined manner. Interest expense for the quarter was $40.9 million, which improved modestly from the prior year. $117.4 million year-to-date, a 12% improvement versus the first 3 quarters of 2025. Wrapping up our 2026 outlook, we are increasing and narrowing our full year core FFO to a range of $1.15 to $1.21 per diluted share up from the prior range of $1.12 to $1.20. This updated range reflects at the midpoint approximately 1 cent of outperformance in the third quarter compared to our initial expectations as well as approximately 1 cent attributable to improved expectations for the fourth quarter.

While we have received no indication that Weil Gotshal will stop paying rent, we have taken a conservative approach and excluded their rent from our guidance for the balance of the year. The 200-basis-point improvement to average in service office occupancy reflect actual results for the first 3 quarters and estimates for the fourth quarter. The 175-basis-point improvement to same store property cash NOI growth reflect the full year estimates of the properties which now contribute to the same-store pool. In both cases, the improved estimates are essentially attributable to sales of 875 Howard and Skyport Plaza, and the temporary removal of Fourth & Traction from the in service and same store portfolio. For seismic upgrades ahead of re-leasing.

After these changes, the company would have likely maintained its prior outlook on both metrics. Arsenal 'track new forward signing' after Mikel Arteta transfer targets revealed

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Your weekly briefing on Arsenal, featuring team news and expert analysis from Matt Verri

I would like to be emailed about offers, event and updates from Evening Standard. Read our privacy notice.

Arsenal have added Antonio Nusa to their list of transfer targets ahead of the January window, according to new reports.

While the Gunners spent big in the summer - adding Ezri Konsa, Bruno Guimaraes and Christos Tzolis to their squad - not bringing in at least one more attacker was considered a risky strategy.

In fact, upon allowing Gabriel Martinelli, Leandro Trossard and Gabriel Jesus to leave permanently, there is an argument that Arsenal ended the summer window weaker in attack than when they started it.

As such, it has been widely reported that the club plan to prioritise signing a forward when the transfer window reopens.

The Mirror, this week, have ‘revealed’ Bournemouth’s Eli Junior Kroupi and Juventus wonderkid Kenan Yildiz are both on Mikel Arteta’s radar, though a January move for either could prove difficult.

Now, the Sun claim the Gunners are also tracking RB Leipzig winger Nusa and have been scouting the Norway international this season.

Predominantly a left-sided attacker, Nusa can also operate on the right flank and has made an impressive start to the campaign by scoring six goals and weighing in with seven assists in 14 appearances across all competitions.

The 21-year-old was on target for Leipzig in their 3-2 defeat to Manchester City in the Champions League on Wednesday night, prompting excitement among some Arsenal fans on social media as a result.

However, the aforementioned report states a deal taking Nusa to north London at the start of 2027 will be difficult to pull off as Leipzig view the winger as a key player and do not want to sell him in the middle of the season.

Furthermore, the Bundesliga club's stance his strengthened after selling Yan Diomande to Real Madrid in the summer for around £120million, meaning they are under no financial pressure to cash in on Nusa.

E-quipment highlight: Jungheinrich launches new forklift for Asian markets

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Like many equipment brands, German forklift maker Jungheinrich has been struggling to crack the Asian industrial markets – but the company is set to change all that with the launch of a new line of 1.6- to 3-ton class electric forklifts designed specifically to navigate the region’s tight warehouses and factories.

Jungheinrich launched the next generation of its EFG MB and MC electric counterbalance forklifts, giving businesses more flexibility to match truck performance with the battery options, charging systems, and operator features that meet their specific needs.

“Different operations have different requirements,” said Lisa Jing Xue, Managing Director at Jungheinrich’s Qingpu Plant, China. “With the EFG M series, customers can configure the (forklift) truck around their application, workload and operators – whether they need greater maneuverability for confined warehouse spaces, or a stable, powerful truck for demanding transport and stacking work.”

Forklift buyers can choose between two performance packages, prioritizing energy efficiency for everyday material handling or more driving and lifting muscle for more demanding, high-throughput operations. The higher-performance package comes standard on models rated for 2.5-3.0 tons, with a choice of either lead-acid or lithium-ion batteries with 346 or 690 Ah of capacity available to suit different workloads and shift patterns. Both ship with regenerative braking tech that can recover up to 30% of the energy used in stopping and lowering loads, too, helping to keep these machines running longer between charging sessions.

“We carried out extensive customer research to understand what the market needs,” added Jing Xue. “Customers can invest in the performance required today and add further capabilities as their application demands.”

Optional features include fingertip controls, a larger 4″ digital display, upgraded LED lighting, and operator-assistance (ADAS) systems, while flexible seating and cabin configurations are designed to keep drivers comfortable through long shifts. A slim B-pillar and glass roof help visibility in busy warehouses, while the four-wheel EFG MC gets automatic cornering speed reduction as standard. Integrated telematics are also standard, giving fleet managers access to utilization data, maintenance planning and pre-operational checks from day one.

Jungheinrich says the order books for both the three- and four-wheel electric forklifts are open now, ready to be supported by the company’s 6,000+ person strong service network across the Asia-Pacific region.

Claude Gets Personal Finance—Anthony Pompliano Accuses Anthropic of Copying His Startup

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Claude Gets Personal Finance—Anthony Pompliano Accuses Anthropic of Copying His Startup

Anthropic launched a personal finance feature for Claude on Thursday, letting users connect bank accounts and ask the chatbot what they have spent, what bills are due, and how much money they have left.

The announcement came with an accusation from crypto investor Anthony Pompliano, who says Anthropic tried to get inside information from his competing startup, Silvia, months before the rollout.

Claude can then summarize spending, spot duplicate charges or unexpected fees, explain interest payments, and build charts or bill calendars. Anthropic's example shows a user asking where their money went this month, then following up to find out what remains before payday.

"Access is read-only, and Claude can't move, manage, or invest your money," Anthropic wrote on X. "Like the rest of Claude, Personal Finance has no ads or sponsored recommendations," the company added.

The assistant can see balances, credit limits, account names and types, the last four digits of account numbers, and up to 24 months of transactions from accounts users approve. According to Anthropic's documentation, conversations inside the Personal Finance hub are not used to train its models, but account balances may lag and AI-generated figures should be checked against bank records.

Also, how Anthropic tracks its users’ financial data and uses it beyond model training is not completely clear—and this is extremely valuable information for big data companies.

Pompliano, whose Silvia product bills itself as a personal AI chief financial officer, posted a lengthy complaint on X shortly after Claude's announcement. He alleged that Anthropic personnel began approaching Silvia engineers in April, supposedly because they were interested in the startup's growth and computing needs.

"Anthropic was fishing for details on how we built our product and why it was so popular," he wrote. Pompliano also described an alleged steak dinner involving Anthropic representatives, claiming someone told the Silvia team that the meal was on CEO Dario Amodei's tab.

His engineers declined to divulge sensitive information, he claimed, adding that someone at Anthropic warned his team about the upcoming launch days earlier.

"Today, Anthropic launched a Claude wrapper that is masquerading as a personal finance product," Pompliano wrote. He also claimed Silvia's computing costs are roughly 97% lower when using its own models and hardware instead of frontier AI models, which most likely mean they are using a local model, likely finetuned for the startup’s specific purpose.

That said, Anthropic was not the first general-purpose chatbot maker to move into personal finance, either. OpenAI launched similar bank connections for ChatGPT in May, also using Plaid for read-only access to financial information, and Agents like Hermes, Open Claw, or Grok Bot can also do pretty similar tasks given the proper skills, permissions, and instructions.

Anthropic says Personal Finance is a first step and is exploring partnerships with licensed professionals and regulated financial firms. For now, the beta is restricted to U.S. customers on three paid Claude plans.

Enhanced Group Acquires Controlling Interest in German Promoter DEAG Deutsche Entertainment

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

The promotion company, which operates festivals and venues across Europe, is valued at $115 million.

Enhanced Group announced Thursday (Nov. 5) that it has acquired approximately a 77% controlling interest in the Berlin-based promotion and ticketing company DEAG Deutsche Entertainment AG.

Enhanced Group will purchase its interest in the German live entertainment company for $8 per share, in a deal that values DEAG at $115 million. The new issues are subject to a lock-up period lasting for up to 12 months, with staggered release dates. Enhanced Group and DEAG expect the transaction to close in the first quarter of 2027.

“I believe one of the most significant long-term consequences of AI and robotics will be a gradual reduction in the need for human labor,” Maximilian Martin, co-founder and CEO of Enhanced, said in a statement. “And I believe people will mostly allocate this free time to shared, real life human experiences. This creates scarcity, as sports and live experiences cannot be automated or replicated by AI. A live game, a concert, a record broken in front of a crowd — all those moments create goosebumps and become more valuable as everything else becomes digitally abundant. We believe the winners will be the companies that own the content, the stage and the ticket. With DEAG, Enhanced will have all three: owned sports and live IP, a broad promoter and venue network, and proprietary ticketing. And we believe we are acquiring it at a very attractive valuation.”

According to a press release, Enhanced plans to evaluate its options for increasing ownership, “potentially up to 100%.”

DEAG has locations in Germany, the U.K., Ireland, Switzerland, Denmark, Spain and Italy, and organizes more than 6,000 live events per year, according to the company’s website. Companies operated under DEAG’s portfolio include DEAG Classics, which focuses on classical music performances; DEAG Concerts GmbH, which has worked with The Rolling Stones; and KBK, which serves as a concert and artist agency serving touring acts including Deep Purple, Lenny Kravitz and Alice Cooper, as well as promoting the W-Festival in Frankfurt, Germany.

The promoter operates festivals including Barcelona’s Rock Fest and AIRBEAT ONE Dance Festival and Indian Spirit Festival, both in Germany. It also owns three venues throughout Europe, including myticket Jahrhunderthalle in Frankfurt, Germany; Salle Métropole in Lausanne, Switzerland; and Arches London Bridge in London.

Incoming CEO Markus Breitenecker will lead DEAG, which Enhanced Group says will continue operating under the existing management team.

Additional reporting by Elizabeth Dilts Marshall.

Jimmy Kimmel, Seth Meyers Call Out Trump’s “I Wasn’t on the Ballot” Excuse for Midterm Losses: “Shut Up”

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

The ABC late night host again took aim at the president and urged him to resign after major Republican losses across the country.

Jimmy Kimmel was quick to take aim at Donald Trump on election night after the 2026 midterms saw significant wins by Democrats across the country.

But he wasn’t done. On Wednesday night’s episode of Jimmy Kimmel Live!, after more results came in and explanations and excuses started streaming in for the blue wave and GOP defeat, the host had more choice words for the president.

Kimmel in particular focused on how Trump claimed, “I wasn’t on the Ballot and if I was, we would have won IN A LANDSLIDE!” Related Stories Movies 'The Fight to Fight' Doc, Criticizing Hegseth's Scrutiny of Women in Combat, Sets Release (Exclusive) Midterms 2026 Six Hollywood Consequences of Tuesday's Democratic Blockbuster

“Oh, shut up. Just shut up. Please. Give us a break for one day and shut that ridiculous mouth,” Kimmel said. “You are such a spoiled child. Did you ever learn anything? About manners — or sportsmanship — or simple common decency? Nothing is ever your fault!”

Kimmel added, “You were on the ballot. You’re like dog hair. You’re on everything. You’ve infected every nook and cranny of our lives.”

Indeed, Trump spent months saying he was on the ballot, or told voters to pretend he was, with The Washington Post finding that he made that declaration at least 63 times before Election Day.

Kimmel wasn’t the only late night host to take issue with Trump’s “I wasn’t on the ballot” about-face.

On NBC’s Late Night, Seth Meyers aired a compilation of the multiple times Trump insisted he was essentially on the ballot in the run-up to the midterms.

“I hate to be the one to tell you this, but you were on the fucking ballot, my man,” Meyers said. “Your name might not have been there, but it’s not like everyone forgot who was president. You did like 30 rallies in the month leading up to the midterms. You wouldn’t shut up. You kept talking about how good senators’ asses were. You made the whole thing about you.”

And even if Trump hadn’t suggested that he was on the ballot, the president endorsed many of the Republicans who lost, Kimmel pointed out.

“All your big handpicked candidates lost! You picked Darline Graham to run in South Carolina; you ran Thom Tillis out of the Senate; you backed that pathetic Ken Paxton in Texas; you went all in on Vivek Ramaswamy, and his disgusting campaign ad; and if you hadn’t picked Mr. Charisma J.D. Vance to be your vice president, Republicans would still have that Senate seat they lost in Ohio last night,” Kimmel said.

Kimmel also took issue with Trump, after “whining about election fraud,” telling The New York Times that he would accept the results.

“How is this even news? You’ll accept the results? Oh, thank you,” he said. “How gracious. Results are not for you to accept or reject — they are results. Like, could you imagine this? ‘Spurs say they will accept loss to Knicks in NBA Finals’ No! They don’t have to accept it! It’s the score!”

Kimmel went on to insist that Trump resign.

“Quit already. Just quit,” he said. “Nobody wants you. Even you don’t want to be president anymore. Take that billion dollars’ worth of crypto, buy an island — maybe [Jeffrey] Epstein’s island! Wouldn’t that be a perfect end to this story?”

And the host indicated he was optimistic that a Vance presidency wouldn’t be that bad.

“And maybe you’re thinking, ‘Well, if he quit, wouldn’t that make J.D. Vance president?’ Well, yes, it would. But here’s the thing about J.D. Vance: A person who flipped from ‘Donald Trump is the American Hitler’ to ‘Donald Trump is the greatest president of my generation’ can flip back. It’s easy when you have no spine. He’s a very good flipper.”

Online Media Pioneer Salon To Shutter: “Our Publishing Model Is No Longer Sustainable”

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Salon, one of the internet‘s earliest publications, is shutting down nearly 31 years after its launch.

The site announced Thursday in a farewell post that it would go dark later today. Salon was founded in the wake of a strike at the San Francisco Examiner when one of the newspaper’s editors, David Talbot, started exploring the publishing potential of the internet. Talbot was joined by Gary Kamiya, Andrew Ross, Mignon Khargie, Scott Rosenberg, and Laura Miller in co-founding the site, which debuted on Nov. 20, 1995.

Initially, the site’s web address was “salon1999.com” because “salon.com” was owned by a hair salon owner in Texas. A staffer later recalled that the salon owner “stubbornly refused to give it up until one of our founders flew down there and, after frank, open and increasingly Mafia-like negotiations, received the precious http://www.salon.com in exchange for a large bushel of Salon stock.” Watch on Deadline Related Stories News Political Ad Revenue Breaks Record For Sinclair, Lifting It To Better-Than-Expected Q3 Results News Midterm Elections: Democrats Take Control Of Congress, Riding Big Wins In Texas, Michigan, Georgia & Maine

The left-of-center site always maintained a San Francisco orientation, but gained a wide audience delivering news and criticicm about politics, culture, ideas and other areas. Along with other digital outlets like Slate, it gained traction through the Bill Clinton years and then during resistance to the George W. Bush administration and the Iraq War. Business-wise, these pioneers were buoyed by the sluggish response to the internet by legacy newspapers and magazines, whose fortress mentality only accelerated their disruption. As internet publishers flourished before the arrival of social media, Salon in those years took in enough revenue to at one point employ nearly 150 workers.

The 2010s and 2020s were far less kind to Salon and other web-based businesses reliant on advertising. In November 2023, privately held media company Find.co acquired Salon.com from previous owners Drew Schoentrup and Chris Richmond.

“The business realities of independent digital journalism have changed profoundly over the years,” Salon’s final post observed. “After careful consideration, we have reached the difficult decision that our publishing model is no longer sustainable. We know this news will be deeply disappointing to many of you, and we share that sadness.”

The article went on to emphasize the main takeaways from Salon’s “remarkable” 31-year run, including “the journalism, the ideas, the voices, the conversations and the community that grew around them. Salon helped demonstrate what digital journalism could be and could become. We hope that legacy will continue to matter long after today.”

Why Ecovyst Stock Topped the Market Today

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

The specialty chemicals company had a beat-and-raise third quarter.

Investors considered specialty chemical manufacturer Ecovyst (ECVT +2.11%) to be a special stock on Wednesday. They traded it up after the company published its third-quarter results that morning, and it closed the day more than 2% higher. That trounced the under 0.4% gain of the bellwether S&P 500 index.

For the period, Ecovyst's sales were just under $309 million, which bettered the year-ago quarter by 51%. However, that total includes sales from Calabrian, a business the company acquired at the end of June.

Getty Images. Image source: Getty Images.

In terms of profitability, net income not under generally accepted accounting principles (non-GAAP, or adjusted) increased by 18% to over $25 million, or $0.23 per share.

With those results, Ecovyst notched a pair of beats on the consensus analyst estimates. The more convincing one was revenue, as those pundits collectively modeled for under $282 million. Their adjusted earnings per share (EPS) estimate was $0.21, meanwhile.

In its earnings release, Ecovyst attributed its organic growth largely to increased export demand, which boosted sales of both regenerated and virgin sulfuric acid.

As for the proximate future, Ecovyst quoted CEO Kurt Bitting as saying, "Given current refining economics, we anticipate high refinery utilization through year-end, and we expect demand across our portfolio to remain healthy for the rest of the year."

In line with that bullish forecast, the company raised its guidance for full-year adjusted earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation, and amortization (EBITDA) to a range of $208 million to $212 million. That's well above both the previous estimate ($195 million to $207 million) and the pre-Calabrian 2025 result of $172 million.

It isn't easy to find a thriving company in the specialty chemicals space these days, and it looks as if Ecovyst has hit the sweet spot with its in-demand products. It also seems to be integrating Calabrian smoothly and quickly. With all that, its rosy forecast is fully believable, making it a stock to watch in its sector.

Amorim blasts dire Milan: ‘We have to improve, otherwise it’s over’

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Ruben Amorim sent a strong message to his Milan players after their shocking 2-0 Europa League defeat to Ferencvaros. ‘We have to improve, otherwise it’s over.’

The coach was visibly stunned by the performance from his squad at San Siro, just days after a completely different approach was taken to beat Inter 3-1 in the Derby della Madonnina.

They didn’t have a shot on target in the first half, and capitulated to a Lenny Joseph brace, allowing several other big scoring opportunities to the Hungarian side.

After his strong words in the post-match interview with Sky Sport Italia, Amorim went even further in the press conference, warning that his entire tenure could be cut short.

“I’m simply saying what I see, because you’re not stupid, and I don’t want to fool anyone,” he said.

“I have every reason to defend the team, so when I speak like this, I take responsibility first and foremost, because the team is my responsibility.

“But we cannot afford to do something like this, especially in the first half, in front of our fans, at our home. We can’t do it.

“We have to improve, otherwise it’s over.”

Amorim had already said that it was clear Milan were “not ready to be competitive” every three days, as they have lost three of their first four Europa League fixtures and risk an early exit from the competition.

“This is my feeling, but I’m not the one saying it. The facts prove it. And today’s game was proof of it, we’re not mentally ready. The technical aspect has nothing to do with it. The rotation has nothing to do with it. You can’t tell me that the starting lineup wasn’t up to the task of winning the game.

“We gave ourselves all the chances we had, especially at the start. I remember certain reactions when we lost the ball: they weren’t at all the same as the ones we saw three days ago, and we’d had four days to rest.”

Some fashion collaborations feel unexpected. Others make so much sense you wonder why they didn’t happen sooner. Parke and Stoney Clover Lane fall firmly into the latter category, and lucky for fans of both brands, they’re doing it again.

It’s not hard to see the appeal. Parke has built a devoted following around its oversized sweatshirts, vintage-inspired denim, and relaxed silhouettes, while Stoney Clover Lane has made a name for itself with customizable bags, pouches, and patches.

Derek Hough And A DWTS Winner Have The Same Prediction About One Star Making The Finale (And I Totally Agree)

After Dancing With The Stars Disney Night, I Have Complaints About Jenna And Harry

Derek Hough Made A Mistake During DWTS' Disney Night, And I Can't Stop Thinking About It

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

They are doing anything but paying this contestant no mind.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

While there are obvious frontrunners on the season of Dancing with the Stars airing on the 2026 TV schedule, I’ve always thought Connor Wood could make a deep run on the show. First of all, never underestimate the power of an internet personality. Secondly, and more importantly, he’s so committed to the process and improving so much, it’s easy to see him making it all the way to the end. It turns out I’m not the only one who thinks this, as both Derek Hough and a DWTS champion have predicted that the man who goes by Fibula online will make it all the way to the end.

I feel like it's a big deal when a judge makes a prediction about Dancing with the Stars, so I’m not taking Derek Hough’s words lightly. During an interview with TV Insider, he boldly claimed that he really thinks Connor Wood and his partner Rylee Arnold will be in the finale, stating:

I believe he’ll be in the final. There’s a glow around them. The two of them, I think they’re such a great partnership.

Wood and Arnold really are a wonderful pair, and it’s very clear they adore each other. They are also very funny and endearing, which makes them so gosh darn likable. That could be part of the reason people are voting for him, and why they’ll continue to. Hough made that point clear too, saying:

People love him; I love him. He’s such a good dude; we have the same friends, and he’s a good man. I love him.

Well, there you have it, Derek Hough really thinks Connor Wood will make it to the end, but he's not the only one.

While Derek Hough saying Wood could make the final is huge, there’s another big name who thinks the comedian has what it takes. That person is Joey Graziadei, Season 33’s winner. Speaking to TV Insider about the podcast host, the man who won with Jenna Johnson said:

I will almost guarantee that he makes the finale. I think he can. I think that he’s the perfect blend of his dancing has gotten better, and I would be shocked if he doesn’t have the votes. He seems online to have a really big army behind him and Rylee. He’s just doing it so well.

Joey went on to say that he thinks people can see how committed Wood is to this process, as well as his improvement. In short, “he is what this show is about.” And you know what? Graziadei would know. He won his season of Dancing with the Stars, and he also came in with very little experience. The Bachelor star brought that up too, explaining:

That’s kind of the route I fell under. No one expected me to be good, and I got better throughout, and I think that’s part of the things people look for on that show.

I think Graziadei makes such a good point here, plus he knows from experience. He wasn’t the person on his season with the most dance experience. However, he was so solid all season; he improved big time, and he won. That’s exactly why Connor Wood could make it to the finale too.

The co-host of Brooke and Connor Make a Podcast has shown massive improvement over the weeks, and the joy he and his partner are getting out of this process is infectious. I mean, I wanted to scream with excitement just like they did when they got their first tens (and an eight from Shirley Ballas, which is high praise from her considering how low she scored other contestants) on Halloween Night.

Also, you can’t look past the passionate fans Wood and Arnold have. He has 1.4 million followers on TikTok, and she has 2.1 million. Their videos consistently get millions and millions of views, and we cannot forget the ultra-viral situation that was launched by him saying he had a crush on Jennifer Lawrence and her replying that she saw him on 8th Avenue drinking a smoothie and he paid her no mind.

All I’m saying here is it’s obvious people ride for Papa (which is a nickname Wood has had to try and explain many times), and that mixed with his commitment and improvement on the show is a recipe made for a finalist.

Now, to see if he can go the distance, you can watch Dancing with the Stars every Tuesday at 8 p.m. ET on ABC or with a Disney+ subscription.

HBO Max's 49-Episode Action Series With Alan Ritchson Is One Of Its Best

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Alan Ritchson stars in an HBO Max series with four seasons that is one of the streaming service's most action-packed shows and features fan-favorite characters. Ritchson has cemented himself as one of the biggest actors in Hollywood thanks to his role as Jack Reacher in the Prime Video action thriller series Reacher. The actor has gone from that project to several action releases.

One of those was Netflix's War Machine, which became one of the most-watched Netflix movies of all time earlier this year, showcasing how the actor's connection with the audience is not only related to Prime Video and Reacher. Before Ritchson became as big of a name as he is today, the actor was already starring in high-profile projects and action-oriented shows and movies.

HBO Max's library of TV shows is filled with releases for fans of the action genre. They do not necessarily have to be straightforward action thrillers like Reacher, but shows with plenty of intense fighting scenes, such as House of the Dragon, Peacemaker, and so on. This is where Ritchson comes in, starring in one of the best TV shows on HBO Max in the action department.

Before he played Jack Reacher, Ritchson became a fan-favorite for playing two characters in live-action DC TV shows. The first of those was a Justice League member, as Ritchson gave life to Aquaman in Smallville opposite Tom Welling's Clark Kent/Superman. After that, the Reacher star moved to another DC character, playing Hank Hall, aka Hawk, in Titans, and the show deserves more attention.

Titans was originally released on the DC Universe app before it moved over to HBO Max, where all four seasons of the superhero series are currently available to stream. With Reacher season 5 only expected to be released in 2027, with no firm release date in sight, anyone looking for an action series where Ritchson gets to brutally beat bad guys can find that in spades on Titans. From the start, the live-action DC series set out to shock viewers and separate itself from other DC adaptations, being dark, gritty, TV-MA, and featuring brutal fight sequences. Even Robin was dishing out rage.

Titans is the adult version of the Teen Titans, and for the many viewers who grew up with the animated series starring that team, characters like Dick Grayson/Robin, Starfire, Raven, Beast Boy, and more will feel familiar, though the series does take all of them in bold and surprising new directions. Batman himself is present in the show, with an older Bruce Wayne being played by Game of Thrones' Jorah Mormont actor Iain Glen.

As for Ritchson, he plays the emotionally complex Hawk, a hero who deals with the pain from all the injuries he accumulated throughout his career as a street-level hero with no powers. Titans allows Ritchson to not only put his towering physique to the test, but also flex his acting muscles with many dramatic moments, emotional conversations, and even a few truly hilarious beats. While Titans is worth watching for Ritchson, the show's perfectly cast DC heroes and the bond they create, exciting costumes, powerful villains, and more, I have to note that the superhero series could have reached a much higher shelf than it did. Given the sheer talent in front of the screen, with actors like Ritchson, Glen, Titus Welliver (Lex Luthor), Brenton Thwaites (Robin/Nightwing), and many more, as well as how the show focused on the most popular DC team besides the Justice League, Titans could have turned out to be one of the universally beloved live-action superhero adaptations.

However, with plenty of confusing decisions, such as nonsensical character deaths, botched finales, and more led to the show becoming divisive. Despite its high Rotten Tomatoes score, which is boosted by the show's final two seasons, where Titans really hit its stride, the DC series holds a lower 67% audience score. Still, action-wise, Titans is easily one of the best TV shows on HBO Max, and Reacher fans will get to see plenty of Alan Ritchson breaking villains' bones and leaving them a bloody mess as Hawk.
