10 Biggest Reveals From Final Fantasy 14's Tokyo Fanfest

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Stephanie Watel is a writer for DualShockers. She has 3 years of experience writing about video games, from news to lists to in-depth guides.

To be perfectly candid, Final Fantasy XIV’s Tokyo leg of the Fanfest circuit ahead of Evercold was a surreal and almost puzzling experience. Most of the expansion’s biggest surprises were already spoiled in both Anaheim and Berlin, while Tokyo essentially served as a clarity-and-elaboration session for much of what we already knew in a basic sense.

However, that isn’t to say that director Naoki (“Yoshi-P”) Yoshida’s keynote wasn’t full of impressively dense information for players to soak in. There was a lot to go around, to the point that Yoshi-P went almost 30 minutes past his allotted runtime for the presentation, on top of saying that it originally came out to almost four hours during rehearsal. That still doesn’t even include the “several” Producer Live Letter streams he also has planned leading up to Evercold’s launch to go over everything even further.

Square Enix veteran Koji Fox says lead writer Natsuko Ishikawa is snowed under and off-limits.

If anything, it’s a perfect metaphor for just how dramatically different Evercold is actually set to be, compared to all previous expansions in the game. Most of the game’s major content is getting a systemic overhaul, and those changes branch out even further, down to some of the smallest details. It’s almost on par with A Realm Reborn’s aptly named “rebirth” of the entire game over a decade ago, without having to shut the entire game down for nearly a year.

All that said, Tokyo Fanfest had plenty of information for players to take with them, and here are the biggest reveals from the entire keynote.

Evercold dropping in the dead of winter in January was poetically on point, and up through Berlin’s Fanfest we’ve known that Final Fantasy 14’s latest and most ambitious expansion yet was set to kick off a busy year for gamers (with the likes of Fable and Final Fantasy 7: Revelation also set for early Spring).

However, at the top of Yoshi-P’s keynote in Tokyo, he announced with regret that Evercold has gotten a three-week delay to late February. Now the expansion will debut on February 23, 2027, with a special Early Access period for those who pre-order starting on February 19.

As for why this is the case, Yoshi-P explained that, initially, Evercold would have been “barely” ready to go in time for their original January release deadline, which going by a three-week delay means that the expansion would have arrived at the tail end of month. However, due to “several issues” they encountered during development, Yoshi-P made the executive decision to impose the delay to ensure those problems are properly resolved before launch.

Despite this, the delay hasn’t impacted when players will get to preorder Evercold, and you can do so for all platforms starting November 4, 2026. Ultimately, given just how much is changing in the game systemically with Evercold, it’s just as well that this delay happened, and January did seem a bit ambitious already. Nonetheless, the rock isn’t getting kicked too far down the street, and the expansion will be in players’ hands soon enough.

On the heels of Evercold’s amended release date announcement, Yoshi-P pivoted straight to the grand reveal of the expansion’s second new combat job. Granted, there was strong speculation already after the many hints dropped at Berlin’s Fanfest that it revolved around something akin to a Hunter or a Falconer, and it was already confirmed that it would be a physical ranged DPS.

Finally, all the theory crafting was laid to rest with the reveal of Zephyr, courtesy of Visna posing as the job’s mascot. As shown in the active demonstration video, the player wields a crossbow weapon while flanked by a magical falcon familiar known as a Nephele. The video displayed various combat abilities, from basic attacks with the crossbow to more striking, magic-imbued ones that have your work together with your falcon to produce some spectacular moves.

What’s more, during a Q&A session on the second day of Tokyo’s Fanfest, Yoshi-P answered a fan’s question about being able to customize your bird companion. It turns out that you actually can thanks to a dual-dye system that allows you to recolor the bird when visiting a “certain NPC” in the game.

While FF14 players knew that changes to gear would be inevitable with Evercold’s new activity system, Yoshi-P surprised just about everyone during the keynote with sweeping alterations to raid gear in particular.

While Seasonal Gear is intended for “everyday” players with a simple optimization routine, Raid Gear comes with a few different new details. The first and most surprising change is that every single person in a raid group will now get their own personal chest drop after completing the duty, rather than having the group roll the dice on a single chest once a week. This will make raiding significantly more efficient (especially for premade groups or “statics”) in lieu of having to farm several runs just for the sake of gearing everyone up for the next raid.

Alongside that, players will also get to edit the “secondary attributes” of raid gear, allowing them to personally customize how their earned gear performs rather than just being stuck with preset attributes. Also, the item level of Raid Gear can be raised twice, and materia slots will unlock upon reaching that maximum item level.

Yoshi-P went into explicit detail in this section of the keynote to explain exactly how the gear upgrading process will work, and while it felt somewhat like a labyrinth of steps and stat numbers, it nonetheless looks like a promising move in the right direction for the game’s raid community in general.

The core that’s tying mostly everything together in Evercold’s revamped gameplay is the all-new Activity Level system. The Berlin keynote gave a soft introduction to this system with a basic explanation of weekly missions that will replace Final Fantasy 14’s traditional daily duties.

During the Tokyo keynote, Yoshi-P tied that back in with the introduction of the Activity Level system, which is how all players will obtain their Seasonal Gear throughout each “season” of the expansion’s patch cycle. As you complete Missions from the long list of available options, you’ll earn Activity Points that in turn raise your Activity Level. As that level goes up, you’ll gradually unlock rewards for your efforts, ranging from gil to emotes to mounts, and then so-called “gear categories”.

Unlocking these gear categories allows you to receive gear pieces of that category (e.g. - accessories) for your jobs. Furthermore, each category is shared across all jobs, so you don’t have to worry about your alt jobs missing out on those rewards. Redeeming those gear rewards is also done via the game’s new character UI dashboard.

Up to this point, the basic understanding that we have about our journey on the Fourth is that most of it will occur on the planet’s Fimm Himnar realmships, or the “Five Firmaments Left to Mankind”. These absolutely massive realmships each pertain to a different element and a certain sector of produced resources that can’t otherwise be obtained on the frozen surface.

Back in the Berlin Fanfest, Yoshi-P provided a detailed look at two of these realmships. One was Naglfar, the Realmship of Water where Visna resides. The other was Hringhorni, the Realmship of Fire where we now know that Bastion-wielding Baldr serves as its foreman.

Here's everything you need to know Final Fantasy XIV Evercold, from new characters, new factions, and how we're able to travel to different shards.

This time around, in-depth looks were provided for two more of these realmships. One was the Realmship of Earth, Sessrymnir, and a walkthrough video gave insight into the ship’s agriculture and mining production areas. Meanwhile, the Realmship of Wind, Skidbladnir, was also shown off in all its glory. Not only is it where the ridiculously adorable Kottr live, but it’s where they work on the production of airships, the main method of transportation on the Fourth.

The fifth and final realmship remains a mystery, which is likely for the sake of story spoilers, and it also remains to be seen what other explorable areas there may be. After all, a traditional Final Fantasy XIV expansion normally has at least six major zones that players can freely navigate, and the five realmships leave at least one of those zones in limbo. The closest guess would be somewhere on the subzero surface of the Fourth, but players will have to wait and see in February.

Alliance Raids aren’t just getting a new collaboration with Neon Genesis Evangelion for Evercold, they’re also getting their own systemic overhaul for the new expansion and beyond. These changes, according to Yoshi-P, reflect a few overarching issues that the content has suffered from over past expansions.

These issues start with skewed runtimes for the game’s entire roster of Alliance Raids. Some are generally more “efficient” to run, while others (e.g. - Tower of Paradigm’s Reach) are notoriously long and thus considered not optimal for dailies, leveling, farming gear, what have you. This gaping divide in runtime has since led to a bias within the player community, and leaves certain Alliance Raids infamously difficult to queue for, much less actually complete.

However, Yoshi-P has sought out a solution in the form of subdividing Alliance Raids into two instanced chunks. The first chunk will consist of the overall raid’s first two bosses (with trash mobs in between), while the second chunk will be the final two bosses in the same fashion. After completing the first half and obtaining your rewards from it, you’ll then be able to queue separately at your own discretion for the second half, complete it, and earn separate rewards from that chunk.

This is to help alleviate the burden of farming endlessly for certain gear items over the course of a 30 minute to 1 hour raid instance, all while also being able to enjoy the content more feasibly with less of a time commitment. Furthermore, Yoshi-P hinted at not having to roll anymore for loot from these raids, with a promise to elaborate more on that closer to Evercold’s launch.

Finally, Alliance Raids get one more big update starting in Evercold in the form of a legitimate “Savage” mode. Influenced by the success of Variant and Criterion Dungeon content, this will be an 8-player mode, rather than the usual lineup of 24 WoLs, and will function more as a boss rush-style instance with two bosses from the original raid set at “Savage Turn 3”-level difficulty. This mode will kick off with Evercold’s Evangelion collaboration raid series.

While many of the game’s existing combat modes are getting their own unique changes, there’s also an entirely new type of combat content debuting during Evercold called the Gauntlet of Power.

As its name suggests, it’s a gauntlet-style instance that functions as a more casual (compared to Alliance Raid Savage, at least) boss rush for players to embark on at their leisure. That is to say, it’s a mode that can be easily accessed from almost anywhere via the game’s overhauled UI. It can be played either with a premade group or a random queue, in much the same fashion as other instanced duties in the game.

This boss rush consists of up to five “floors” for a light party group, and clearing each floor leads to rewards that weren’t yet revealed during the keynote. Players can vote whether or not to continue the gauntlet, which provides a flexibility that not many of the other types of battle content in the game have. Despite being simple in concept, it may very well help bridge a gap that’s been lingering in Final Fantasy’s 14 gameplay flow for several years.

What’s more, this mode will help keep things interesting by choosing from an assortment of 200 different existing bosses in the game. This assures that the mode will traditionally prevalent issue of content monotony as much as possible while maintaining a sense of liberty and gratification for the player to make the most of their time.

Another big surprise that Yoshi-P was surprisingly candid about during Tokyo Fanfest’s Q&A session is that Allagan Tomestones of Poetics, the currency that’s remained in the game the longest (aside from maybe MGP), is set to officially retire in Evercold.

Yoshi-P did say that he shouldn’t really be discussing it just yet, but did confirm the plan nonetheless. He did offer a brief explanation in that “there will be something that allows you to trade for gear for alt jobs, or past rewards; you don’t have to worry about it for now. It just won’t be Poetics anymore.”

For players who have been in Final Fantasy 14 for at least some amount of time, Allagan Tomestones has since become the fall-back currency for the game’s more niche purchases. Alternatively, you could redeem them for one of the game’s more recent variants of tomestone currencies. Beyond A Realm Reborn, Poetics lose much of their remaining purpose, and at this point they feel more like a souvenir that just collects dust from one expansion to the next.

However, on that note, with the game’s dramatic revamp to the Activity Points system and Seasonal Gear, it remains to be seen if other tomestone currencies, such as those we had in Dawntrail, will keep some sort of utility themselves. Yoshi-P hasn’t been abundantly clear on just how long tomestone currency will hang on for, and if it will perhaps deviate to some other set purpose in the game. Otherwise, Evercold may be the “poetic” farewell to tomestone currency across the board as we know it.

While not necessarily anything to do with the game’s many different content systems, the keynote did get a small update on the story front in the form of identity reveals for certain characters. Baldr, the one wielding Bastion gear in the trailer, was revealed as the foreman of the Hringhorni realmship. Meanwhile, an entirely new face appeared in the final version of the trailer that ironically looked very familiar to many fans.

The presentation revealed her as Lagertha, the first mate of the Naglfar realmship where Visna resides. However, beyond finding out her purpose on the ship, fans are moreso scratching their heads over her striking resemblance to Ysayle, a key character who tragically passed during the events of Heavensward and the Dragonsong War.

However, players with a sharp eye may have caught the glimpse of her during the final Dawntrail MSQ content in Patch 7.56, meaning that her appearance is no coincidence. The most likely explanation for her presence on the Fourth is that Lagertha is her reflection. As to how deeply her character will impact the story of Evercold, players will simply have to wait and look forward to it.

On another non-combat tangent, Final Fantasy 14’s ever-controversial housing system is getting more bells and whistles added to it with Evercold’s patch cycle. Those changes are spread across “three pillars”, as indicated in the presentation.

First off, as hinted over the past several months, all houses will get an “interior area size customization” feature. This means that, for example, you can customize the interior of your small house to have the layout of a large house, for a currently undisclosed gil fee. Not only that, the furnishing limit will flex according to the layout you choose, so there’s no need to worry about getting cut off on your desired decor.

Secondly, floating interior furnishings will no longer be something only achievable through unconventional PC glitching controls. Along with an all-new placement interface, players will be able to move furnishings more freely on the vertical axis on both console and PC platforms.

The third and final pillar consists of adding even more housing plots, though Yoshi-P noted that they’re still discussing how exactly they want to achieve that, whether it’s through more, smaller areas, “hidden” areas, et cetera. While more plots feels moreso like another bandaid to a system that itself needs an overhaul, it’s at least nice that they’re doing their best to improve what’s in place until that change hopefully comes one day.

An updated version of the Xbox PC app available to Xbox Insiders loads up to 60% faster, and it's got some other nice improvements too.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Xbox has joined the 'faster launcher load times' arms race, announcing that its updated Xbox PC app, available now to Xbox Insiders, launches up to 60% faster, and has also been overhauled to offer "simpler navigation, updated filters, and new ways to organize your games."

Along with the faster launch time, Xbox said the home, store, and Game Pass pages in the updated app load up to 50% faster, while in-app windows open up to 65% faster—depending upon factors including device, internet connection, system configuration, and usage, of course. Library navigation has been simplified, search has been improved, and friends and social features are now part of the main navigational window.

Credit where it's due: That change to friends and social features is welcome. I don't use the Xbox App much—to be blunt, it's pretty much exclusively for Sea of Thieves. But after a long stretch away from it, I recently jumped back in for some high seas adventures with the crew, and I'll tell you this: Figuring out how to connect with my fellow online swashbucklers was kind of a headache compared to the super-simplicity of Steam. It worked fine, it just wasn't smooth and easy, so anything that helps with that, I'm happy to take.

I'm less excited about the faster loading times. Not that I'm going to complain about performance improvements, I'm just not sure it matters in the grand scheme of things. When the Epic Games Store promised that its "vibe coded" launcher was faster than Steam, a common reaction was mockery: Much of that was sparked by Epic's insistent banging of the 'AI is inevitable' drum, but there was also a lot of 'who cares?' in the reactions, too. I think the Xbox PC app may face the same: Faster is good, sure, but is it going to convince many PC gamers to use it instead of Steam when they don't have to? Of this, I have doubts.

(Steam also recently dropped a performance update that promises to improve loading times as well, blunting the impact of both the Epic and Xbox boosts even further.)

If you want to check out the updated app for yourself, you'll need to be in the Xbox Insiders program—for that, you'll need to install the Xbox Insider Hub. A "refreshed Xbox web experience" is also on the way, with an improved library tab, more efficient use of screen space, and light, dark, and high-contrast visual modes.

What to Expect From Best Buy's Black Friday and Cyber Monday Sales This Year

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

For all intents and purposes, Black Friday sales are already here. There are plenty of deals out already, even though we're weeks away from the actual event. One of the reasons for that is the competition retailers have with each other to be the first to get your money. One of the retailers that is making the biggest early splash is Best Buy, confirming their entire plan for the rest of the year. Here's what you need to know.

Best Buy has announced the following events:

DoorBoosters: A Halloween sale from Oct. 30 to Nov. 1.

Early Black Friday Deals and Doorbusters: The official start of early Black Friday deals on Nov. 6. New deals will drop every Friday.

Black Friday Doorbusters and Deals: Best Buy says this will be its main sale, starting on Thursday, Nov. 19 and running until Nov. 28 with deals on "computing, gaming, home theater, wearables, and more."

Cyber Sunday and Monday: Best Buy is extending Cyber Monday to five days and says you can find deals going up to 50% off select tech.

Last-minute savings: The Christmas sale will run from Dec. 18 until Dec. 24.

You don't need to be any kind of member to shop Best Buy's Black Friday in July sale; however, it is free to sign up for a My Best Buy membership, which will get you free shipping. If you join one of their paid subscriptions (My Best Buy Plus for $49.99 per year or My Best Buy Total for $179.99 per year), you'll get faster free shipping, exclusive prices and deals, an extended 60-day return window on most products, and access to select sales, like the Member Deals Days sale that is going on right now. Paid members will also earn extra rewards from both the current sale and the upcoming Black Friday in July sale.

You can find deals on TVs, laptops, headphones, video games, electric bikes, and more. There will be new deals every week in stores, on its website, and in the Best Buy mobile app. They'll offer price matching, meaning if the price drops further during the sale (Dec. 24), they will match it and offer a refund.

Minnesota United vs San Jose Earthquakes - prediction, team news, lineups

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

By Joshua Ojele | 05 Nov 2026 22:31, Last updated: 05 Nov 2026 22:49

Minnesota United and San Jose Earthquakes draw the curtain on their 2026 Major League Soccer regular season campaign when they lock horns at Allianz Field on Sunday morning.

While the home side head into the league finale needing maximum points to stand a chance of securing back-to-back playoff appearances, San Jose will aim to finish the campaign strong and gather momentum ahead of the post-season quest.

A strong showing at the business end of the season has put Minnesota United in contention for playoff qualification ahead of a crunch final-day showdown, with the Loons securing a 3-1 win over Los Angeles FC on Monday to maintain their newfound form.

Joaquin Pereyra turned in an eye-catching display at the BMO Stadium, netting on either side of Cherrion Valerius’s 34th-minute strike to put the visitors three goals up, before Sergio Vasquez pulled one back for Los Angeles FC in the 89th minute.

Coming off a 3-1 victory over Sporting Kansas City on October 29, Minnesota United have now picked up three wins in their most recent four outings and are unbeaten in six straight matches (3W 3D), having lost each of the five games preceding this run.

This late-season resurgence has seen Cameron Knowles’s men surge into 12th place in the Western Conference standings with 41 points from 33 matches, just two points behind ninth-placed Colorado Rapids in the final playoff qualifying spot.

Attacking improvements have been integral in Minnesota’s upturn in form, with 2+ goals scored in five of their last six games, and they will also hope to make home advantage count against an opposing side who have failed to win their last six league visits to Allianz Field, losing four and claiming two draws since a 3-1 victory in May 2018.

Having seen their form take a dip in recent weeks, San Jose Earthquakes finally rediscovered their winning touch last Saturday when they held on to see out a 2-1 victory over Real Salt Lake and record their first victory since late September.

In an action-packed first half at PayPal Park, Ousseni Bouda and Benjamin Kikanovic netted in a 15-minute spell to put the Quakes in a commanding lead, and after Juan Sanabria halved the deficit with a 23rd-minute strike, the home side did well to repel Real Salt Lake and leave with all three points.

Prior to that, San Jose saw their nine-game unbeaten league streak come to an end on October 25, when they were hammered 3-0 by FC Dallas at the Toyota Stadium, four days before playing out a 2-2 stalemate with Colorado Rapids on home turf.

After enduring a disappointing 2025 campaign that saw them finish 10th in the West and narrowly miss out on the playoffs, Bruce Arena’s men have enjoyed a significant turnaround this season, collecting 52 points from 33 games to sit third in the conference standings, three points behind second-placed Dallas.

With playoff qualification already assured, San Jose will be keen to finish the regular season on a high, but a run of just one win in eight matches since August suggests they still have work to do before the campaign concludes.

Minnesota United Major League Soccer form:

San Jose Earthquakes Major League Soccer form:

Experienced midfielder Wil Trapp has missed each of Minnesota United’s last three matches due to the MLS concussion protocol, and the 33-year-old will play no part in Sunday’s finale.

American youngster Kayne Rizvanovich was left out of the team list against Los Angeles FC last time out, and the 19-year-old goalkeeper is also expected to sit out this weekend's matchup.

Kelvin Yeboah has been a standout performer for Minnesota this season, and the Italian striker, who has racked up 18 goals and four assists in 29 league starts, will be one to keep an eye on.

San Jose Earthquakes will be without several players down the spine of the team, including Darius Johnson and Jonathan Gonzalez, who have been ruled out through injuries.

Nigerian forward Nonso Adimabua is currently recuperating from a muscle problem while DeJuan Jones continues his lengthy spell on the sidelines through an Achilles tendon injury.

Minnesota United possible starting lineup:

Callender; Valerius, Diaz, Duggan, Markanich; Chancalay, Triantis, Gene, Gonzalez; Pereyra, Yeboah

San Jose Earthquakes possible starting lineup:

Edwards Jr.; Kikanovic, Munie, Roberts, Romney; Harkes, Fernandez; Bouda, Tsakiris, Ricketts; Judd

Buoyed by their victory over Real Salt Lake, San Jose Earthquakes will head into the season finale in high spirits, but they have their work cut out against an in-form Minnesota United side who have their sights set on a top-nine finish.

However, San Jose’s form on the road leaves a lot to be desired, and we predict Minnesota will outscore Arena’s men this weekend and extend their impressive home record in this fixture.

Scott Parker’s dramatic West Ham return leaves him struggling for words

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

The former midfielder with impressive managerial promotion record began with a thrilling comeback in draw at Cardiff

Three days in and Scott Parker has already lost his voice. West Ham’s head coach arrived on Tuesday, watched his side fight back from 3-0 down to draw with Cardiff on Wednesday and was struggling to make himself heard when he met the media on Thursday afternoon.

“It’s like in the summer holidays,” Parker said. “You put the flip-flops on for five weeks, then you come back home and you get your shoes on. You’re in a minute, you’ve got a blister on the back of your heel. It’s the same with a throat. I’ve not shouted for a little while. All of a sudden there’s shouting and it’s a bit croaky.”

Parker is back in the thick of it. Out of work after leaving Burnley in the wake of relegation from the Premier League last April, he cut short his break from management when West Ham parted company with Nuno Espírito Santo. Nuno was sacked early on Tuesday and replaced by Parker, who is intent on leading West Ham out of the Championship at the first time of asking.

It will not be easy. West Ham occupy the final playoff spot after three games without a win and displayed their inconsistencies against Cardiff. They made another slow start, conceding three during the first half, but roared back after a triple half-time change from Parker. Jarrod Bowen was inspired. But Parker needs complete performances if West Ham are to win automatic promotion.

“The Championship’s a tough league,” he said. “It doesn’t matter what name you have on a bit of paper. You have to have a level of humility of what this league needs. That’s a challenge when you come out of the Premier League.”

Parker argued that talent was not enough. Burnley were stingy when they won promotion. Parker has to sort out West Ham’s defence. “Of course we want to play a certain brand,” the 46-year-old said. “But I think it’s just a non-negotiable in terms of a committed team.”

Connections need to be rebuilt with disillusioned supporters. Parker was an all-action midfielder who loved his four years at West Ham as a player. He has added Mark Noble to his backroom staff. Noble has held the role of West Ham’s sporting director since retiring in 2022 but Parker confirmed the former club captain would have a role as an assistant coach. “He’s going to be coaching with me and be that sounding board,” Parker said.

Noble will be able to get Parker up to speed on his squad. West Ham need improvement at the back, better balance in midfield and more bite up front. Nils Koppen, the director of recruitment, could be busy in January. Parker wants to give everyone a clean slate, though. Soungoutou Magassa and Edson Álvarez will have opportunities. Even Maxwel Cornet travelled with the squad to Cardiff.

Parker did not focus during negotiations with Karim Virani, West Ham’s interim CEO, and Daniel Kretinsky, the club’s largest shareholder, on whether funds would be available for signings. One step at a time.

West Ham are in transition. David Sullivan still owns a 40% stake but is no longer on the board. Kretinsky’s power has grown, although the Czech billionaire is yet to say much in public. Parker has heard more from Kretinsky than most people.

“I found a man that was hugely passionate,” Parker said. “I instantly felt that, along with Karim, along with Nils. These were three people who know where they want the club to go.”

The target is a return to the top flight. Parker has won promotion with Bournemouth, Burnley and Fulham but his record at keeping teams up is less impressive. He has been dismissed a little unfairly as nothing more than a promotion specialist.

“In a short space of a coaching career I’ve had some huge success,” Parker said. “I’ve had some blips in terms of two full seasons in the Premier League. All I know is that I’m fully committed.”

That mindset convinced Kretinsky, Virani and Koppen to pick Parker. They wanted his knowledge of the club and record at this level.

West Ham are eighth before visiting Blackburn Rovers on Saturday. They are taking 7,000 fans to Ewood Park and Parker knows there can be no more complacency.

Jordan Rodgers Makes Rare Comment About Brother Aaron After Family Reunion

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Jordan Rodgers is — surprisingly — rooting for his brother Aaron Rodgers’ playoff chances after their family reunion.

During a Wednesday, November 4, appearance on ESPN, Jordan, 38, was asked which AFC starting quarterback he would trust the most going into the playoffs. (Aaron, 42, leads the Pittsburgh Steelers.)

“This one might surprise you a little bit,” Jordan began on the broadcast. “I’m gonna go Rodgers, and here’s why … [because] the Steelers know who they are. They have an elite defense that’s playing at a high level and they run the freaking ball, so they’re asking less of Aaron than they ever have at any point in his career, right? He needs to manage the run game, he needs to find opportunities to get his playmakers … in space.”

He continued, “I just feel like I trust the guy that’s got the MVPs, that has the veteran status that’s seen everything that’s also not required everything of him for them to win. I think that’s a good sweet spot they’re in.”

The brothers had long been estranged ever since Jordan revealed to now-wife JoJo Fletcher on a 2016 episode of her season of The Bachelorette that Aaron would not be present with the rest of their family due to long-held drama. Aaron later criticized his sibling for publicizing their familial issues.

Aaron, thanks to his mysterious wife Brittani, ultimately reconciled with his parents, as well as brothers Luke and Jordan earlier this year.

“There was some stuff there and then I could tell I was coming to a time where I needed to make it right,” Aaron said on the “Not Just Football” podcast in September. “My parents were wanting to move to Nashville to be closer to their grandkids, and there were some tragic events that happened in Chico, [California] that kind of made them not feel as tied to it.”

Aaron traveled to California to mend fences with his parents, Ed and Darla, before hitting the road to speak with Luke, 44.

“A couple weeks after that, I met with my little brother. It was cool,” he added of Jordan, revealing the siblings had a “tough conversation” together. “I’m in a much better headspace, and I said it before, but my wife deserves a lot of the credit for that just to be even able to to get to that point where I could think about having the conversation.”

Weeks later, Jordan made a rare comment about Aaron while appearing on ESPN’s First Take.

“I think if you truly watched Week 1, this was a team that was a few drops and a few drops away from being absolutely explosive like we haven’t seen out of the Steelers in a really long time,” Jordan said of the Pittsburgh team’s season start. “Rodgers was actually very accurate down the field. The throws that were not completed because of either a non-call or a drop down the field were on the money. Not trying to be a homer.”

FC Barcelona: Joan Laporta’s poignant tribute to Lionel Messi

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

This Friday, Lionel Messi won the Princess of Asturias 2026 prize in the Sport category. This distinction rewards his exceptional career in football, but also his human influence and his sporting heritage. An emblematic figure of FC Barcelona for years, the Argentinian remains deeply linked to the Catalan club, with which he made history thanks to his numerous titles and his remarkable performances.

FC Barcelona also welcomed this recognition through a message addressed to its former player via Joan Laporta, its president: “Leo, congratulations on receiving the 2026 Princess of Asturias Award in the Sports category, a well-deserved recognition of your footballing and human career. Thanks to your talent, you helped make FC Barcelona, ​​the club of your life, even greater, yesterday, today and forever. Messi and Barça, Barça and Messi: a shared success story of which we, the loyal Barça supporters, are deeply proud and which we hope will be eternal.»

Charlton Athletic vs Sheffield United - prediction, team news, lineups

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

By Sam Varley | 05 Nov 2026 22:29

Aiming to continue their climb up the Championship table and break into the playoff places, Sheffield United will head to The Valley to take on Charlton Athletic on Saturday.

Back-to-back victories have seen the visitors move within a point of the top eight, while their visitors sit four points above the drop zone after four straight losses.

Charlton Athletic return to action at the weekend aiming to end a dismal run of Championship form and arrest a drop towards the relegation zone.

Following a 19th-placed finish in their first season back in the second tier last time around, the Addicks won their first three league matches of the new term but have since drawn five and lost six of 11 matches.

In those games, Nathan Jones's side have scored just six goals and conceded 20, dropping to 21st spot in the process, just one place and four points above Burnley in the drop zone.

They have now suffered four straight defeats heading into their final match before the international break, falling to Watford, Norwich City and Middlesbrough before they hosted newly-promoted Bolton Wanderers on Tuesday and again came away empty-handed as Sam Dalby scored the only goal inside 20 minutes at the Valley.

With pressure surely growing on Jones, and the international break arguably coming at a decent time to arrest Charlton's slump, they will be desperate to put a win on the board to increase their lead over the drop zone ahead of the return to action.

Their visitors, meanwhile, head to London aiming to carry an upturn in form into the break.

Unlike the hosts, Sheffield United are now bouncing back from a slow start to the Championship season, having set out to challenge for promotion this term only to pick up just nine points from their first eight matches before the opening break of the season.

Chris Wilder's men have reversed their fortunes since, though, putting 13 more points on the board in six outings courtesy of four wins and a draw alongside a solitary away defeat to Portsmouth.

Following a 1-0 home victory over Wrexham on Friday, the Blades headed to strugglers Preston North End on Tuesday and came from 1-0 down to win 3-2 thanks to Sydie Peck's first-half hat-trick, leaving them on 22 points from 14 matches with just five teams conceding fewer than the 17 goals they have allowed.

Now in a much better position in ninth spot, and just one point behind eighth-placed West Ham United, Sheffield United will look to make it three straight wins with the chance to head into the second break of the campaign in the playoff places.

Charlton Athletic remain unable to call on winger Rob Apter, who has been sidelined since early September.

In their midweek defeat, Jones replaced midfielder Nathaniel Chalobah with attacker Miles Leaburn at half time and could go with a more attacking shape from the outset at the weekend with Leaburn, Matt Godden, Sonny Carey and Millenic Alli all competing for attacking spots.

That is after Charlie Kelman led the line against Bolton with support from Harvey Knibbs, Karlan Grant and top scorer Tyreece Campbell, while Conor Coventry is a mainstay in the engine room and Will Mannion was preferred in net over Arthur Okonkwo last time out.

Sheffield United are without forwards Patrick Bamford and Ryan One and midfielder Joe Rothwell, while Kalvin Phillips was also absent in midweek but may be fit to feature.

He is unlikely to be rushed back, though, and Wilder should field a similar starting XI from their midweek away win, meaning Jairo Riedewald may again partner Peck, who decided that game with his hat-trick, while Liam Kitching should come straight back into the defence after serving a one-match suspension on Tuesday.

In a 4-4-2 shape, they may again be flanked by Romelle Donovan and Tahith Chong with Ben Brereton Diaz, Tom Cannon, Tyrese Campbell and Oliver Arblaster competing for attacking spots in Bamford's absence.

Charlton Athletic possible starting lineup:

Mannion; McNamara, Jones, Koumetio, Bell; Coventry; Alli, Knibbs, Grant, Campbell; Leaburn

Sheffield United possible starting lineup:

Manchester United have made contact with former France manager Didier Deschamps as the club continues to explore managerial options amid growing uncertainty surrounding Michael Carrick’s future.

That is according to a report from French outlet Foot Mercato, which claims that Manchester United board leaders have reached out to Deschamps in recent days to see if he is interested in taking over at Old Trafford.

The Red Devils’ hierarchy is actively sounding out elite profiles to stabilise the club during a chaotic Premier League campaign.

The report adds that while United have also made approaches to Germany boss Julian Nagelsmann and Aston Villa’s Unai Emery, who appears set to sign an extension with the club, Deschamps has emerged as a big name target.

However, the report further adds that the 58-year-old is in no rush to get back into management and is not too keen on taking a job at Manchester United due the club’s current situation.

Deschamps brings one of the most decorated managerial resumes in international football, having guided France to FIFA World Cup glory in 2018 and a dramatic runners-up finish in 2022.

The former midfielder, who experienced the Premier League firsthand as a Chelsea player during the 1999-2000 season, spent 14 trophy-laden years in charge of Les Bleus before stepping down.

Having previously led Monaco to a UEFA Champions League final and Marseille to a Ligue 1 title, his proven track record of managing superstar egos makes him an enticing option for Old Trafford decision-makers.

That said, it is not surprising that he’s shying away from the role at Old Trafford given that things at the club might need fixing beyond managerial changes.

The contact with Deschamps comes at a time where the position of current head coach Michael Carrick remains uncertain

Despite securing a thrilling 3-2 comeback victory over AS Roma in the UEFA Champions League,  the miraculous win has done little to guarantee Carrick’s long-term security.

Sitting near the bottom half of the Premier League table after an agonising winless run, Carrick faces immense pressure, with United’s executive board preparing contingency plans should results fail to rapidly improve.

Celtic cure insomnia before subs make the difference to spark Europa League lift-off

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Goals from the bench as Celtic come from behind to claim 3-2 win over Besiktas

Celtic are up and running in the Europa League at the fourth time of asking after a crucial 3-2 home win over Besiktas.

After losing their opening three games, Celtic needed a positive result against the Turkish side and showed fortitude to come back from a goal down to breathe life into their aspirations of reaching the knock-out stages.

They were thankful for supersubs Callum Osmand and Camilo Duran, who came on to rescue the match for Celtic in an action-packed second half following Besiktas' 54th-minute opener. The win leaves Celtic in 32nd place with three points at the halfway stage of the main phase.

Celtic travel to Torreense of Portugal and Cypriot outfit Omonia later this year before matches against Marseille (home) and Union (away) in January. They can head into those fixtures with some optimism after a much-needed victory, only their fourth in 11 matches across all competitions.

Manager Martin O'Neill came into this match under pressure following Sunday's 2-0 defeat by Falkirk. The 74-year-old rotated his team, giving rare starts to right-back Jordan Lotomba and winger James Forrest, and introduced his key players at the right time.

The first half could have been bottled up and used as an insomnia cure. Celtic had one shot and two legitimate touches in the Besiktas penalty box. The Turkish side did little of note either, their former Rangers winger Vaclav Cerny routinely jeered.

Mercifully, the second half was far better.

Besiktas took the lead on 54 in calamitous circumstances. Celtic goalkeeper Sam Johnstone walloped a clearance off Leandro Trossard and the ball squirted to Ernest Poku. He absolutely skinned Lotomba, who chopped down the winger with his flailing attempt to salvage the situation. It was a clear spot-kick and while Johnstone partially redeemed himself by saving Olaitan's powerful penalty, the ball spun into the sky and towards the goal, where Olaitan followed up to tap home.

Lotomba tried to recover with a surging run from his own penalty box on 59 minutes that got the home crowd briefly energised. It was his last act, replaced by Colby Donovan on the hour mark. Duran and Osmand were also brought on for Forrest and Sebastien Tounekti.

Osmand did more in the first minute of action than the powderpuff Tounekti did in 60. The Englishman immediately made an impact, with Callum McGregor seizing on a loose ball and feeding the forward to find the bottom corner of Alexander Nubel's goal with an accurate strike. Celtic had something to build upon.

Besiktas had played comfortably up until this point but now looked a little rattled. Celtic embarked on their best period of the match. Nubel reacted smartly on 67 minutes to save a netbound Liam Scales header. Benjamin Nygren whistled an effort just over the bar eight minutes later. Suddenly the game was there for Celtic.

The ineffective striker Kasper Hogh was subbed off for Haissem Hassan as O'Neill turned to the Egyptian to unlock the Besiktas defence. But it was two earlier subs who made the difference again on 77 minutes, with Osmand slipping Duran in for the Colombian to round Nubel and send Celtic Park wild.

Celtic now had to hold on but their resistance was pierced by a vicious strike from Besiktas sub Orkun Kokcu from 25 yards on 86 minutes that fizzed beyond Johnstone and into the net. Never mind, though, when Duran is in town. The Colombian took in a sclaffed cross from Kieran Tierney a minute later and netted.

And this time, it was job done. Celtic Park roared its approval at full time.

Garth Brooks Sexual Assault Accuser Wins Court Ruling in Bid to Stay Anonymous

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

The ruling overturns an earlier decision that noted Brooks had already revealed her name: "We are not persuaded that the proverbial cat is out of the bag here."

A woman who accused Garth Brooks of sexual assault can potentially keep her real name hidden, a federal appeals court has ruled.

The U.S. Court of Appeals for the Fifth Circuit overturned a trial judge’s ruling last year that Jane Roe must use her real name in the litigation, which centers on her accusations that the country star assaulted her while she worked for him as a hairstylist.

The lower judge had ruled that her need for privacy was “weakened” because the “cat” was already “out of the bag” after Brooks publicly disclosed her name in court filings and she accidentally included it in another. But in its decision Wednesday (Nov. 4), the appeals court said that’s not how it should work.

“We are not persuaded that the proverbial cat is out of the bag here,” the appeals court wrote in the ruling, obtained by Billboard. “Internet sleuths might speculate that Roe is a particular person [but that is] not the same as a public broadcasting of Roe’s certain identity.”

Neither side’s attorneys immediately returned requests for comment on Thursday (Nov. 5).

The accuser filed her claims against Brooks in Los Angeles court in October 2024, claiming he exposed her to “appalling sexual conduct,” including an alleged incident in which she says he raped her during a May 2019 stay in a Los Angeles hotel room. Brooks has vehemently denied the allegations, calling them “lies” aimed at forcing him to pay “hush money.”

Weeks before that case was filed, Brooks had quietly filed his own preemptive lawsuit in Mississippi federal court under a John Doe pseudonym, accusing an unnamed Jane Roe of extorting him with threats of a public lawsuit. After she filed her case in Los Angeles, Brooks refiled his own lawsuit with her real name listed.

The move quickly sparked outrage from the accuser’s attorneys, who immediately moved to reseal her name and seek legal penalties against Brooks. They also asked the media not to disclose the name, and Billboard abided by that request.

Last year, the judge overseeing the federal case in Mississippi ruled that Roe was not entitled to proceed under a pseudonym. He cited the fact that her name had already been disclosed, both by Brooks and in an inadvertent filing in the California case.

But in Wednesday’s appeal decision, the Fifth Circuit said those disclosures did not mean Roe was less entitled to use a pseudonym in a case dealing with issues of the “utmost intimacy.”

“As to the fact that Roe’s name was a matter of public record for short periods, that was either because of Doe’s actions (which Roe objected to), or Roe’s inadvertent disclosures which counsel corrected,” the appeals court wrote. “In sum, we agree that Roe’s privacy interest … should not be weakened for these reasons.”

The ruling was not an outright reversal, and did not order the lower court to allow Roe to keep her name shielded. Instead, it merely said the trial judge analyzed the situation incorrectly and should reconsider in light of the appellate court’s decision: “We express no view on the ultimate outcome of this issue.”

The ruling might finally allow for progress in the litigation over the accusations against Brooks, which has been largely stalled for more than two years and remains in the earliest stages. The Mississippi case will proceed first, followed by the Los Angeles lawsuit.

Taylor Sheridan's 'Terminal List' Replacement Is Officially a Streaming Hit

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

If Chris Pratt fans can't get enough of The Terminal List's military operations gone wrong, off-the-books missions, and powerful people secretly pulling the strings, Paramount+'s Lioness is your next watch. Created by Taylor Sheridan, Lioness exposes the darker side of serving one's country, where those on the front lines can be blindsided by greater interests that put not only their lives at risk but also the people around them.

Instead of a Navy SEAL commander, Lioness stars Zoe Saldaña as senior CIA case officer Joe McNamara, who also leads the covert Lioness program. Based on a real CIA initiative, the Lioness program sends female operatives to infiltrate the inner circles of designated targets by getting close to the women surrounding them. With the latest season of Lioness officially racking up an impressive 3.9 billion minutes viewed within just eight weeks, there's all the more reason for The Terminal List fans to check out the series.

In the aftermath of a failed Lioness mission in ISIS-controlled Syria that leaves her undercover operative dead, Joe (Saldaña) faces scrutiny from the CIA for taking drastic measures that nearly expose their involvement. Tasked with finding a replacement, she settles on Cruz Manuelos (Laysla De Oliveira), a Force Recon Marine turned Lioness operative assigned to get close to the daughter of an Iranian terrorist financier.

The show that claimed the most of your answers is the world you were built for. If two tied, both are shown — you're complicated enough to straddle two Sheridan universes.

You are a Dutton — or you might as well be. You understand that some things are worth protecting at any cost, and that the modern world's indifference to history, to land, to legacy, is not something you're willing to accept quietly. You lead from the front, you carry your family's weight without complaint, and when someone threatens what's yours, you don't escalate — you finish it. You're not cruel. But you are absolute. In Yellowstone's world, that combination of ferocity and loyalty doesn't make you a villain. It makes you the only thing standing between everything that matters and everyone who wants to take it.

You thrive in the chaos of high-stakes negotiation, where the money is enormous, the margins are thin, and the wrong word in the wrong room can cost everyone everything. You're a fixer — the person called when a situation is already on fire and needs someone with the nerve to walk into it. West Texas oil country rewards exactly what you are: sharp, adaptable, unsentimental, and absolutely clear-eyed about what people want and what they'll do to get it. You're not naive enough to think this world is fair. You're smart enough to be the one deciding who it's fair to.

You are a Dwight Manfredi — someone who has served their time, paid their dues, and arrived somewhere unexpected with nothing but their reputation and their wits. You adapt without losing yourself. You build loyalty through respect rather than fear, though you're not above reminding people that the two aren't mutually exclusive. Tulsa King is for people who are still standing when everyone assumed they'd be finished — who find, in an unfamiliar place, that they're more capable than the world gave them credit for. You don't need a throne. You build one, wherever you happen to land.

You carry the weight of a system that is broken by design, and you do it anyway — because someone has to, and because you're the only one positioned to do it without the whole thing collapsing. Mike McLusky's world is for people who are comfortable operating where there are no good options, only less catastrophic ones. You speak every language: law enforcement, criminal, political, human. That fluency makes you invaluable and it makes you a target. You've made your peace with both. Mayor of Kingstown belongs to people who understand that keeping the peace is not the same as being at peace — and who do the job regardless.

Over the seasons, Lioness follows the tense working relationship between Joe and Cruz, with the former putting the latter through hell and back to prepare her for the assignment. But Cruz isn't the only operative to join the Lioness program. Season 2 introduces Captain Josephina Carrillo (Genesis Rodriguez), who's assigned to infiltrate a cartel. However, Season 3 takes a different turn from the usual Lioness formula, with Joe herself getting caught in direct danger and having to hope that the CIA reaches her in time before the worst happens.

Lioness isn't afraid to get its hands dirty. In The Terminal List Season 1, Reece's world falls apart after his platoon is ambushed during a mission and his wife and daughter are murdered in a separate attack. Driven by grief and a thirst for revenge, he compiles a target list of everyone responsible and sets out to kill them. As for Lioness, Joe doesn't have any personal vendettas, but the loss of her previous Lioness operative, partly due to her failure to properly vet them, and the mission's ultimate failure motivate her to hit back twice as hard. She needs someone willing to endure the program's downright problematic and highly triggering training methods, but Joe also has no qualms about sacrificing the operative herself if things go south.

However, despite Joe's seemingly cold-shouldered attitude toward her agents, which reflects the objective, no-mercy nature of CIA work, she is also a family woman. With a husband and children of her own, running a covert CIA program that the government refuses to acknowledge means her family has little protection if things go wrong. Given how many enemies Joe has made throughout her career, it's only a matter of time before her family, who already disapprove of her CIA work, becomes a target. And even Joe can't guarantee that she'll always be able to protect them.

It turns out that Kayce can't fully forget his past, after all.

While much of The Terminal List Season 1 follows Reece's personal quest for vengeance, Season 2 sees Reece pulled back by the CIA to stop a resurgent Al-Qaeda network. The same goes for Lioness, which follows Joe and her team as they conduct counterterrorism operations both inside and outside American borders. Joe believes her work is meant to protect innocent people from potential threats. However, both shows also question how much control these operatives actually have when their missions are linked to larger agendas.

If these thriller shows have shown anything, it's that politics and business often go hand in hand. Just as Reece discovers a conspiracy involving people who put his team in danger for their own financial gain, Joe learns in Lioness that terrorism isn't always an isolated threat. Behind these attacks are wealthy third parties who use terrorist groups to manipulate global economics and advance their own interests. With so much at stake, Lioness expands on what The Terminal List also explores — how national security can become a game played by people with too much power, leaving the soldiers on the ground the ones who must risk their lives to deal with the consequences.

Special Ops: Lioness is a Paramount+ original series starring Zoe Saldana and Nicole Kidman. The series centers on a marine and a CIA agent who work together with the daughter of a dangerous terrorist group to destroy the organization. Taylor Sheridan (Yellowstone co-creator) and Jill Wagner created the series, which was directed by Paul Cameron and Anthony Byrne.

Allan Saint-Maximin Leads Very Different Life Away From Football

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

In England, Premier League fans will best remember Allan Saint-Maximin for his time at Newcastle United between 2019 and 2023. These days, the French winger plays for MLS club Charlotte FC, but – away from football – he has a very different life going on at the same time.

Saint-Maximin scored 13 goals and claimed 21 assists in 124 games for Newcastle. More than his stats, though, the winger caught the eye with his electric style of play. This helped him earn a £23m move Al-Ahli in 2023, but he's career since then has been that of a journeyman.

He was loaned out to Turkish side Fenerbahce after just one season in Saudi, and then joined Club América in Mexico one summer later. By February, he was sold to Lens, but only months later joined Charlotte in the United States.

With two goals and four assists in his first 15 games for the MLS club, he's settling in steadily enough, but the 29-year-old had reasons away from football to be content with his prospects right now anyway.

The 28-year-old has issued a statement following his sudden exit on Saturday.

As revealed in an interview with The Athletic, Saint-Maximin recently had the chance to make millions from his venture outside of football. The business in question is known as Helios, which is a Greek mythology-inspired card game created by the former Newcastle man.

The MLS star has claimed that he received a £22m (€26m) offer for the business, but turned it down as he wants to continue growing his free-to-play game. He explained:

“The journey is not about money. It’s to create something, to create jobs for people, to create opportunities.”

Saint-Maximin added that, despite rejecting the offer, he would prefer to bring in others to work alongside him, each taking a stake in the company in return for their investment.

It's not the first time he's spoken about Helios, named after the sun god. In 2022, he explained how he grew up with an interest in Greek mythology. This inspired him to combine his love for board games with the topic and create the game. He would even take it on team buses when travelling to away games.

"When I was young, I learned about Zeus and Athena and saw a lot of films and anime movies with them in," he said. "When you start to understand it, you really love it.”

According to Helios' official rulebook, the aim of the game is to win by earning coins or stealing them from your opponent. Saint-Maximin explained how he developed it, adding: “There was a lot of work, a lot of nights, because you need to come up with a new idea.

"I tested it with friends. We started to play small tournaments at home. It took two and a half years — but I am really happy that I didn’t stop.”

These days, his company appears to be going from strength to strength, and could even one day put him among footballers who have become successful businessmen

Atlassian shares fall 17% as slower subscription growth forecast overshadows earnings beat

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Shares in Atlassian Corp. fell more than 17% in after-hours trading today on a forecast for slower subscription growth from the Australian collaboration software company.

Its fiscal 2027 first-quarter results beat analyst estimates. The stock had nearly doubled between Atlassian’s August report and late October.

For the quarter ended Sept. 30, Atlassian reported adjusted earnings per share of $1.65, up from $1.04 in the same quarter of the previous year, on revenue of $1.772 billion, up 24% year-over-year. Analysts were expecting $1.43 per share on revenue of $1.71 billion.

Most of the growth came from cloud, where revenue rose 31%, to $1.302 billion. Customers kept adding Jira and Confluence seats in the quarter, according to the shareholder letter, including in business teams well outside software development.

Growth in Data Center, the self-managed product line Atlassian will retire in March 2029, slowed to about 5%, leaving revenue there at $391 million. The company said those customers are expanding less as they plan their moves to the cloud.

Subscription annual recurring revenue came in at $6.812 billion at the end of September, up 22.5%. Remaining performance obligations jumped 44%, to $4.746 billion. Enterprise customers are behind that increase, according to the company, with deals getting larger and stretching over several years.

Atlassian made money on an unadjusted basis for the second quarter running, with net income of $91 million, or 35 cents per share. Chief Financial Officer James Chuong put that down to keeping costs in check while revenue grew. Free cash flow climbed to $463 million on strong collections, and Atlassian returned $581 million to shareholders through buybacks.

On the product side, more than 87% of enterprise cloud customers now use Rovo, the company’s artificial intelligence assistant. Upgrades to the Teamwork Collection bundle rose sevenfold from a year ago to more than 3,000 customers. Annual recurring revenue from the larger Service Collection bundle is now above $1 billion, spread across more than 70,000 customers.

Chief Executive Mike Cannon-Brookes used the earnings release to argue that enterprise AI has reached “an inflection point.” Model power alone no longer sets anyone apart, he said. Platforms that give people and AI agents shared context to work from will pull ahead by his account, and Atlassian has “never been better positioned” to be one of them.

For the second quarter, Atlassian expects revenue of $1.825 billion to $1.835 billion, a little ahead of the $1.82 billion analysts had forecast, with cloud revenue growth of about 27%.

Most of Atlassian’s fiscal 2027 targets edged higher in the update. The company now sees full-year revenue growth of about 13.75%, three-quarters of a point above its August forecast. Because customers pulled Data Center purchases into fiscal 2026 after the retirement announcement, this year’s revenue comparisons are tougher.

Total revenue growth should pick up again in fiscal 2028, Atlassian said. Investors sold the stock on a different number. The company expects subscription annual recurring revenue growth to slow to about 18.5% by fiscal year-end, down from the 22.5% it just reported.

Perplexity launches its own take on AI agents for work

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Like OpenAI’s Dots and Claude Duet, Perplexity’s Teammates can perform an array of tasks while running 24/7.

Like OpenAI’s Dots and Claude Duet, Perplexity’s Teammates can perform an array of tasks while running 24/7.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Perplexity is taking on OpenAI and Anthropic with its own work-focused AI agents. The startup’s new always-on “Teammates” can take on multiple roles within a workspace, while pulling existing projects, skills, and memory from the Perplexity tools you already use.

Teammates integrate with Perplexity Computer, an AI system that can deploy agents on your device to complete tasks. But Perplexity says Teammates “build on top” of Computer by offering the ability to collaborate and complete “ongoing, large-scale work.” You can assign each Teammate different jobs that they can perform simultaneously — much like OpenAI’s Dots and Claude Duet. Perplexity’s take on the feature is much more expensive, however, as it requires a subscription to its $200 / month Max plan.

Perplexity notes that you can keep tabs on Teammates’ progress through individual threads in the Perplexity app, as well as interact with them in Slack. “Over time, we envision every org having hundreds of teammates working for each person,” Perplexity CEO Aravind Srinivas says on X.

Glyph Continues Its Expansion into Creator Support with New Card Readers

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Glyph Continues Its Expansion into Creator Support with New Card Readers

Earlier this year, Glyph pushed hard into the creator space with the launch of new SSDs and a line of CFexpress memory cards. It is continuing that trend with the announcement of memory card readers.

The series includes two new additions: the Glyph Capture+ CFexpress Type A USB4 Memory Card Reader and the Glyph Capture+ Dual SD Memory Card Reader. The company says these are designed to meet “the rigorous demands of modern film, photography, and audio workflows” and combine its SteadyIO technology (which is Glyph’s term for fan-less thermal sinking) with support for SD and CFexpress Type A cards, specifically targeting Sony photographers and filmmakers.

While Glyph currently doesn’t make any SD cards or CFexpress Type A cards, it recognizes that Sony photographers represent a huge part of the market. As such, the Glyph Capture+ CFexpress Type A USB4 Memory Card Reader promises what it characterizes as next-generation offload efficiency thanks to USB4 Type-C (40Gbps) connectivity. It can achieve transfer speeds of up to 5,000 MB/s, which Glyph says allows for data ingest up to 3x faster than traditional readers.

The Glyph Capture+ Dual SD Memory Card Reader features two SD card slots and can function as a dual-ingest device. But that versatility does come at a slight speed cost: this reader uses USB 3.2 Gen 2 (10Gbps) connectivity with transfer speeds up to 1,000 MB/s. That is likely fast enough for most photographers using SD cards in their workflows, since the cards can’t operate at speeds any higher than that anyway.

The readers are constructed of a solid aluminum shell with an increased thermal ceiling and a protective soft composite bumper. Compact and bus-powered, Glyph says both models fit seamlessly into desktop and field setups across Mac and Windows environments.

Both the Glyph Capture+ CFexpress Type A USB4 Memory Card Reader and the Glyph Capture+ Dual SD Memory Card Reader will begin shipping in November, but availability may slip into December depending on demand. Both readers will be available directly from Glyph and authorized resellers. The Dual SD reader retails for $120, while the Type A reader will cost $150—the same price as Glyph’s existing Type B reader.

From Defense to Detective: How to Watch Jalen Brunson Make His ‘Law & Order: SVU’ Debut Tonight

Celtic player ratings v Besiktas: game-changers produce late fireworks as trio of starters struggle

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Marks out of ten for Celtic players after crucial 3-2 victory on matchday four of the Europa League

There were fireworks aplenty at Celtic Park on Bonfire Night as Martin O’Neill’s side produced a stunning late turnaround to claim a dramatic 3-2 victory over Besiktas and finally get their Europa League campaign up and running.

For long spells, this looked destined to be another miserable European evening for the Scottish champions. A dismal first-half performance was followed by Jordan Lotomba’s penalty concession and Junior Olaitan’s opener, leaving the Hoops staring down the barrel of a fourth consecutive league phase defeat.

However, the second-half introduction of Callum Osmand and Camilo Duran changed the complexion of the contest, with the substitutes turning a seemingly lost cause into a memorable comeback victory.

Osmand equalised before Duran struck twice, including an 87th-minute winner, after Orkun Kokcu had threatened to spoil the party with a stunning long-range effort.

It was a result Celtic desperately craved. Their European campaign had been in danger of unravelling after three successive defeats, following on from their failure to negotiate Champions League qualification against LASK. O’Neill had previously insisted there should be no ceiling on his side's continental ambitions, but with the games beginning to run out, this was effectively a must-win encounter.

Besiktas arrived in Glasgow in considerably better shape, sitting eighth in the 36-team standings after victories over Marseille and Hoffenheim and a draw with Crystal Palace. Celtic, meanwhile, were looking to avoid four consecutive defeats in major European competition for the first time since December 2017.

There were familiar faces in the visiting ranks, too. Vaclav Cerny returned to Parkhead following his controversial water-bottle incident involving Celtic supporters during Rangers’ 3-2 victory in March last year. Former Hoops striker Oh Hyeon-gyu also featured, while ex-Rangers defender Ridvan Yilmaz was named among the substitutes.

Fireworks provided some pre-match spectacle, but the atmosphere inside Parkhead was subdued, with a sparse home support reflecting the growing discontent surrounding the club.

The opening exchanges offered little to lift the mood. Celtic struggled to build any real momentum, while the loudest reaction from the home support came whenever Cerny found himself in possession.

The visitors gradually assumed control as Celtic struggled to keep hold of the ball. By the 39th minute, O’Neill's men had registered just two touches inside the opposition penalty area. Nygren's air shot inside the Besiktas box shortly before the interval provided an unfortunate summary of Celtic's attacking frustrations.

Celtic's evening took a turn for the worse in the 52nd minute. Jordan Lotomba, on his first start at right-back, made a rash sliding challenge on Poku right on the edge of the penalty area, with the contact occurring on the line. The referee had little hesitation in pointing to the spot.

Johnstone initially produced an excellent save to deny Olaitan, but the goalkeeper was left helpless as the Besiktas forward reacted quickest to convert the rebound.

At that stage, Celtic looked short of ideas and confidence, with the prospect of a fourth straight European defeat looming large.

Then came the turning point. O’Neill introduced Osmand, and the youngster needed just 43 seconds to make his mark. McGregor threaded a delightful reverse pass into his path, allowing the 20-year-old to take one touch before drilling a composed finish into the bottom corner.

The goal sparked an immediate transformation. Celtic suddenly had purpose and aggression, with the crowd responding to the renewed energy on the pitch. Scales went close to levelling matters when his deflected header was superbly clawed over the crossbar by Nubel, while Nygren subsequently dragged another opportunity narrowly wide after being picked out by Osmand.

Celtic's second goal felt increasingly inevitable. It arrived in the 78th minute, with Duran providing the finishing touch to another excellent attacking move. Having been shifted into the central striking position, the Colombian immediately demonstrated what he could offer through the middle, timing his run perfectly to latch onto Osmand's pass, round Nubel and slot into the unguarded net.

The hosts looked capable of adding to their tally in the closing stages, but Besiktas had one final response. Captain Orkun Kokcu, introduced from the bench, unleashed a sensational effort from around 25 yards that flew into the bottom-left corner, leaving Johnstone with no chance.

It was a devastating blow so late in the contest, but Celtic were not finished. Just two minutes later, Duran once again found space behind the visitors defence as Tierney's effort fell invitingly into his path. The Colombian showed excellent footwork to bring the ball under control before stabbing it beyond Nubel and restoring Celtic's lead.

This time, there would be no way back for the visitors. There is still plenty of ground to make up in the Europa League standings, but Celtic have at least given themselves something to build on with this result.

We give each player a mark out of ten for their display.

Godzilla Minus Zero Has Two Cameos Only Hardcore Franchise Fans Noticed

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

The following post contains brief spoilers for "Godzilla Minus Zero."

"Godzilla Minus Zero" is pure Godzilla spectacle. This is a bigger, badder king of the monsters that's not without its flaws, but nevertheless offers enough spectacle and kaiju action to make it a thrilling cinematic experience. Sure, we've had plenty of kaiju movies the past decade, but few manage to get anywhere near the scope or scale that Takashi Yamazaki offers in his follow-up to the Oscar-winning "Godzilla Minus One."

One of the things that makes this movie special is how it expands the world of the previous Godzilla. By bringing in King Ghidorah (whose design unfortunately leaked online before the film's release), the movie throws open the doors to all sorts of Toho kaiju. It's significant that it's Ghidorah who becomes Godzilla's first foe here, because the three-headed alien dragon that shoots gravity-affecting lightning is such a weird creature that it allows for pretty much anything to follow him. From here, you could easily do Mechagodzilla, or Mothra, or even Gigan.

That being said, "Godzilla Minus Zero" remains a surprisingly grounded and human movie even if it deals with extraterrestrial threats. This is a film with some of the most harrowing kaiju imagery in decades, as it shows how truly horrific the aftermath of a Godzilla attack would actually be. It's unlike any Godzilla movie we've had in this new era. This feels like classic Godzilla without trying to reinvent the wheel, and it feels more grounded than the bonkers and silly MonsterVerse.

Which is why it's funny that this movie features two cameos from two very different corners of the Godzilla franchise — cameos only hardcore fans will notice. Specifically, the film features cameos from directors Hideaki Anno and Jordan Vogt-Roberts.

Hideaki Anno is a legend of anime, having created the groundbreaking and hugely influential "Neon Genesis Evangelion." But Anno is above all else a massive nerd who's spent the past decade bringing every one of his childhood obsessions to life on the big screen, starting with "Shin Godzilla," and then with "Shin Ultraman" and "Shin Kamen Rider." Anno's "Shin Godzilla" was a revelation, a political satire about the failures of government to respond to a crisis that also happened to feature a terrifying Godzilla.

Anno has a blink-and-you-miss-it cameo in "Godzilla Minus Zero" when Koichi and Noriko leave Tokyo and are on a train south. The camera lingers for less than a second on a man reading a newspaper containing news of Ghidorah's attack. That man is the legend behind "Shin Godzilla."

Jordan Vogt-Roberts' cameo comes at the very end of the film when a group of American soldiers capture professor Murakami. What makes his cameo interesting is that Vogt-Roberts has not actually directed a Godzilla project. Instead, he's best known for directing "Kong: Skull Island," which is a very fun take on a kaiju movie and is part of the MonsterVerse, but doesn't actually feature the King of the Monsters himself.

Speaking with The Hollywood Reporter, director "Godzilla Minus Zero" director Takashi Yamazaki explained how Vogt-Roberts ended up in the final film.

"Jordan makes quite frequent trips to Japan. During one of his trips, he reached out to us, and we set up a dinner," Yamazaki said. "That's when he said, 'Hey, I'd really love to be in the next Godzilla movie you're working on.' And I said, 'Well, if you're in Japan, perhaps there's a scene we could work you into.'"

EA employee stumbles on "nearly 20,000" game assets in a storage room, leading to 7 new remasters

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →
